# ⭐⭐⭐ RULINGS — **cross-cutting canon. Lean on purpose.**

> ⭐⭐ **Code answers *"how it IS."* ⛔ It can NEVER answer *"how it was MEANT to be."*** ⇒ **that answer
> lives in the DESIGN docs** *(`docs/blueprints`, now free of handoffs/reports)* and, for generic process
> rules, in **`.claude/CLAUDE.md`**.
>
> ⛔⛔ **This file is NOT a feature index.** It was, and it rotted and bloated. It now holds only the three
> things a design search and CLAUDE.md do NOT give you:
> **① engine invariants that fail silently · ② cross-subsystem hazards · ③ decisions that span features.**
>
> ⭐ **For any feature question: `grep -rln docs` and read the owning DESIGN doc** *(`R-129`)* —
> ⛔ do not expect a row here. Every quote below is verified verbatim by `scripts/rulings-check.py`.

---

## 0. ⭐⭐⭐ How to use this file

| when | do |
|---|---|
| ⭐⭐⭐ **session start / after compaction** | **READ THIS WHOLE FILE** *(it is short)* + `.claude/CLAUDE.md` |
| ⭐⭐ **a FEATURE design question** | ⛔ **do not look here first** — `grep -rln docs` for the feature, read the owning `DESIGN_*` / `Architect_Question_*_ANSWERS` doc *(`R-129`)* |
| ⭐⭐ **before a handoff item / architect question** | ⛔ **cite the owning DESIGN doc + section** in `design-basis`, not a row here |
| ⭐ **when you find a CROSS-CUTTING hazard/invariant/decision** | ⭐⭐ **add a row** — ⛔ but a feature ruling belongs in its DESIGN doc, not here |

### 0a. ⭐⭐⭐ RULING ID BLOCKS — one number range per lane *(user, `2026-10-12`)*

> 🔒 **User:** *"let's pls also change the rules for numbering the rulings, similar to numbering issues (using number ranges) so
> different lanes do not collide."*

| lane | block | next free *(update when you allocate)* |
|---|---|---|
| `ui` | **`R-1000` – `R-1999`** | `R-1025` |
| `behaviors` | **`R-2000` – `R-2999`** | `R-2072` |
| `backend` | **`R-3000` – `R-3999`** | `R-3049` |
| `coordinator` *(added `2026-10-15`, user: "give coordinator a range")* | **`R-4000` – `R-4999`** | `R-4067` |
| *a new lane* | the next free thousand: the SAME thousand as its `CE-` block in the tracker's "ID blocks" table; **add its row here first** | |

- ⭐ **The same thousands as the tracker's `CE-` blocks**, so a lane's ids are recognisable on sight (`CE-2130`, `R-2001`).
- ⭐ **Inside a block, plain incrementing numbers.** ⛔ No letter suffixes on NEW rows.
- ⛔⛔ **Never renumber.** `R-01` … `R-282` stay exactly as they are; the collisions before the blocks keep their **lane tag**: `R-204 (backend)` / `R-204 (behaviors)` · `R-269 (ui)` / `R-269 (backend)` · `R-271 (ui)` / `R-271 (backend)` / `R-271 (behaviors)` · `R-272 (backend)` / `R-272 (behaviors)`, and the behaviors rows `R-273 (behaviors)` … `R-282 (behaviors)`. Quote the tag with the id.
- ⭐ A row from a block needs no tag: the number says the lane.
- ⚠ **Probes:** the `probes` block lists the id exactly as the row writes it (`R-2001`, or `R-271 (ui)` for an old tagged row).


---

## 1. 🔴 SILENT-FAILURE HAZARDS & STANDING CONSTRAINTS — **cross-subsystem; a design search will not surface these**

| id | ⭐ the ruling | source |
|---|---|---|
| ⚠ **R-07** | ⛔ **MOOT for AI variables since `2026-10-13` (`CE-3193`): a blackboard variable carries no `Scope` (nor `Role`); `WorkingStateScope` survives only as the slot-key formula, and `Entity` went with `CE-441`.** *Was:* ⚠⚠ **UNRECONCILED — `DESIGN_Parameter_Model.md` (marked AUTHORITATIVE, `2026-08-16`) gives ONE three-valued `Scope` `{Node,Behavior,Entity}`, contradicting this.** ⛔ **Reconcile before acting.** As ruled by `Q-b` *(`2026-08-13`)*: **`Scope` is NOT cross-host** — blueprint `{Asset, Graph}` = **visibility**; AI `{Node, Behavior, Entity}` = **blackboard slot sharing**. `Q-b`: *"No. `Asset` and `Graph`, and stop there"* | `Variable_Model_Unification.md` |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-21** | ✅ **LIFTED `2026-08-26` (user): visual checks are NO LONGER suspended — but scoped SMALL because the headless harness carries most of it.** ⭐⭐ Per **R-124** the ImGui layer is **railable headlessly** *(`ui-probe` in-frame measurement — `GetContentRegionAvail`/`IsPopupOpen`/`GetItemRectSize`, no image compare)*, so the render/interaction defect class *(width, un-drawn modal, reopening `[x]`)* is caught by RAILS, not eyes; reserve eyes for the subjective residual only. ⛔ **HISTORY:** ~~NO VISUAL CHECKS until the Details panel is implemented AND the emitters and all access infrastructure are unified~~; `VISUAL_CHECK_Guide.md` was SUSPENDED *(user, `2026-08-14`)*. ⚠ I ran one anyway on `2026-08-17` — the user re-derived that ruling unaided | `Q32_…_ANSWERS.md` |
| 🔴🔴 **R-24** | ⛔⛔ **Field order must be preserved within each group — or every LIVE blueprint state slot is HARD-RESET.** 📐 **Measured `2026-08-18`, `BlueprintTickSystem.cs:92`:** every tick compares `slot.StructureHash` to the compiled `def.StructureHash`; on mismatch it calls **`ResetSlot` + `InitDefault`** ⇒ ⭐ **accumulated runtime state on that entity is discarded and replaced by declared defaults.** ⭐ **NOT silent** — `_logSink.OnHardReset(blueprintId, entity, oldHash, newHash)`. ⚠ *"Deployed"* means **live entity state in a running or loaded world**, ⛔ not a release artefact | `BlueprintTickSystem.cs:92` |
| 🔴🔴 **R-63** | ⛔⛔ **A DIRECT WRITE TO `ActiveView` WHILE PAUSED IS LOST ON RESUME.** 📐 **Measured `2026-08-18`:** `OnHit` captures `_postTickSnapshot ← _liveRepo` then rewinds `_liveRepo ← _preTickSnapshot`; `ActiveView` **is** `_preTickSnapshot` while paused; ⭐ **`RequestStep`/`RequestContinue` restore `_liveRepo ← _postTickSnapshot`**. ⇒ ⛔ **ruling 15's *"the command buffer may be UNNECESSARY — write directly to the view"* is MEASURED FALSE.** ⭐ **The ECB staging path is REQUIRED** — the restore overwrites the whole repository, so anything written into the rewound view is erased. ⚠⚠ **STATE CLAIM CORRECTED `2026-08-22` by `W5`** *(📌 §M: a state claim rots; the DECISION above does not)*: this row used to add *"…and THEN drain"*. 🔴 **The resume path no longer drains** — the drain is the kernel's `PreFrame` `ResumeAndDrainSystem`, a PULL *(`R-126`)*, which is what lets a TOOLBAR pause's edit land at all. ⭐ The restore stays, and the ordering argument is unchanged: the drain runs on a later advancing frame, i.e. still after the restore. ⚠ **REFINED `2026-08-26` by `CE-035`:** the restore in `RequestContinue` is now **guarded by `_isPaused`**, and the RESUME half is unconditional — ⭐ the restore only ever made sense while the world was rewound, so this row's claim is unchanged for the paused case; ⛔ do not read it as *"`RequestContinue` always restores"* | `DataBreakpointManager.cs:495` |
| ⚠ **R-65** | ⛔ **`Blackboard1024` is ONE component SHARED by BTree, HSM and Blueprint at disjoint offsets** ⇒ a whole-component write **clobbers other subsystems' state**. ⚠⚠ **The size argument I used twice — *"exceeds `MaxComponentSize`"* — is FALSE** *(`1024 > 1024` is false; it fits exactly)*. ⭐ **Cite the sharing, never the size** | `Q32_…_ANSWERS.md` |
| 🔴🔴 **R-88** | ⛔ **NARROWED `2026-10-13`: the `Scope=Entity` case is gone (`CE-440`/`CE-441`), variables no longer carry `Scope`/`Role` (`CE-3193`), and params are one named DTO in `Params` (`CE-2135`) — what remains load-bearing is a working-state variable's name (slot key `FNV(assetId ++ name)`), a DTO field name the order JSON sets, and a blueprint declaration.** *Was:* ⭐⭐⭐ **A VARIABLE NAME IS LOAD-BEARING AT RUNTIME IN FOUR CASES, AND EDITOR-ONLY OTHERWISE — do not treat rename as one feature.** 🔴 `Scope=Behavior` *(slot key = `FNV(assetId ++ name)`)* · 🔴🔴 `Scope=Entity` *(slot key = `FNV(name)`, **cross-asset** via `TryGet/TrySetShared`)* · 🔴 **any variable a scenario overrides** *(`ParseParams` step 2 switches on `case "{name}"`)* · 🔴 **any blueprint declaration** *(`StructureHashComputation` appends `f.Name` ⇒ `R-24` hard reset)*. ⭐⭐ **The promoted BTree/HSM `Role=Input` params case is NOT among them** — offsets come from the packer and the thunk key is `MethodFqn@offset` ⇒ **editor-only** | `DESIGN_Variable_Details_And_Editing.md` §5 |

| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-137** | ⭐⭐⭐ **UNIFICATION MAY NOT COST A FEATURE. If collapsing N implementations into one takes a capability away, that is a SIGNAL to put it back — as CONFIGURATION — not an acceptable trade.** 🔒 **User, `2026-08-28`, verbatim:** *"we should not lose flexibility of the features, if unification takes some aways, this is a singal we should think how to put it back (via configuration for example)"* ⇒ ⭐⭐ **Every unification slice owes an explicit answer to *"what could each host do before that it cannot do now?"*** — ⛔ *"the hosts are now the same"* is not that answer. ⭐ The intended mechanism is the **per-instance configuration seam** the settings ruling already gives *(standalone store, injected per host, shared between hosts, or `Empty`)* — 📌 worked example: `UXI-23` `S1` keeps `MapLayerAssignmentSystem`'s **injectable definition set** so the layer MECHANISM unifies while WHAT the layers are stays configurable *(`S4`)*. ⚠ **This is NOT a licence to keep duplicate implementations** — ruling 9 still binds. ⭐ The distinction: unify the CODE, parameterise the BEHAVIOUR. |

## 1c. ⭐⭐⭐ HARD LIMITS & PERSISTENCE PROMISES — **break these and it fails silently**

| ✅ **R-39** | ✅✅ **RESOLVED, and the thing the ruling was ABOUT no longer exists.** 📐 The contradiction was a doc-vs-code one over the width of `BrainBlackboard.BehaviorParameters`. ⛔ `P4` deleted that component; a behaviour's params are its **root params slot**, sized exactly `RootParamsAccess.RootParamsBytes(def)` and seated by the partition allocator. ⭐⭐ **And the cap inverted:** `CE-307` repointed the analyzer at `BehaviorConstants.MaxRootParamsByteSize` = `BlueprintTierLadder.Tier16384PayloadSize` = **16 096 B** — a **capacity bound**, not the 100-byte corruption guard it was. The guard existed because params sat inline with neighbours after them; there are no neighbours in a slot. ⚠ **`MaxBehaviorParamByteSize = 100` still exists and is NOT a cap** — it is the reservation width for a behaviour that declares a `ParseParams` and neither a manifest nor a layout type, the documented escape hatch, with one production reader. ⛔ Still do not size a param DTO from memory — **run the analyzer** | `BehaviorConstants.cs` · `BehaviorParameterSizeAnalyzer.cs:39` |

## 2. ⭐⭐ CROSS-CUTTING DECISIONS — **recent; span features or subsystems, so they live here not in one design**

| id | ⭐ the ruling | source |
|---|---|---|
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-124** | ⛔⛔⛔ **THE IMGUI LAYER IS RAILABLE — `R-21`/`R-62`'s PREMISE IS FALSE HERE.** *(user, `2026-08-20`: "of course imgui can render, you have virtual frame buffer xvfb")* 📐 **PROVEN, not argued** — the stack is **Raylib-cs 7.0.2 + rlImgui-cs 3.2.0**, the machine has **Xvfb + Mesa software GL**, Raylib ships `TakeScreenshot`, and a probe **reproduced the live Batch-99 defect and verified its fix**: an `AlwaysAutoResize` popup with a `WidthStretch` column measures `GetContentRegionAvail().X` = **60.0 px** *(the clamp floor — `InputInt`'s step buttons eat it)*; one `SetNextWindowSize` ⇒ **305.0 px** and the value renders. ⇒ ⭐⭐⭐ **the strongest form needs NO image comparison** — measure inside a real frame *(`GetContentRegionAvail`, `GetItemRectSize`, `IsPopupOpen`)*, which catches the width defect, the un-drawn modal and the reopening `[x]` alike. ⚠⚠ **`R-21`/`R-62` STAY IN THE LEDGER** — ⛔ everything built under them was correct AT THE TIME; ⭐ what changed is the measurement, not the judgement. ⛔ **From now on a UI defect is REPRODUCED before it is fixed** | `tools/ui-probe/README.md` |
| ⭐⭐ **R-125** | ⭐⭐ **EVERY MODAL CLOSES ON `ESC`.** *(user, `2026-08-21`, on the variable edit dialog: "can be cancelled by [x] closing button (but not by ESC - any modal needs to be)")* ⭐ **A general rule, not a fix to one dialog** — ⛔ a modal that traps the designer is a defect whatever it is about. ⚠ **`ESC` must do what CANCEL does** — 📌 the same lesson as the `[x]`: ImGui clearing its own flag is not enough while the SESSION stays open, or the popup reopens on the next frame *(Batch 100 `100c`)* | `RULINGS.md` |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-126** | ⭐⭐⭐ **ONE SOURCE OF "PAUSED" — THE CLOCK — AND THE TICK LOOP DRAINS.** *(user, `2026-08-21`: "the final real source is just one - the (1). All others should be derived from this (1) and likely they should not be latched/cached as the state should be read from the original source all the time")* ⭐ **(1) is `GlobalTime.TimeScale == 0`** — 📐 already a DERIVED property on an ECS singleton pushed every tick. ⛔ **The debugger pause is a CAUSE, not a second source**: it switches time mode *(cluster-wide eventually — 🔒 "now just inside the editor process")*, and the clock reports the result. ⇒ ⛔⛔ **an OR-ing predicate over the arms is the THIRTEENTH notion, not the fix.** ⭐⭐ **And staged writes drain from the SIM TICK LOOP** — *"on the first next simulation tick… maybe in first brain non-frozen tick"* ⇒ ⛔ **PULL, not a release event: no path can forget to raise what is never raised.** ⭐⭐⭐ **Corollary the user stated directly — *"I do not understand how comes that something can be unwritable… we should be able to write anything anywhere"*: RUNNING IS NOT A REASON TO REFUSE, IT IS A REASON TO STAGE.** ⇒ `RefusedRunning` and `LiveWriteRefusal.NotFrozen` are **deleted**; ⚠ only DATA-shaped refusals survive *(no entity · field not resolvable · size mismatch — 📌 the last is `Q32` §2.1's corruption gate and must stay)* | `Architect_Question_48_What_Stopped_Means_And_Who_Drains.md` §5 |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-127** | ⛔⛔⛔ **THE INTEGRATION NET COMES FIRST — NOTHING IN THE TIME REFACTOR STARTS UNTIL IT IS GREEN.** *(user, `2026-08-21`: "the integration tests are the most important thing we need to make working before we touch any time monitoring/control related code. lets put that as the beginning of all the tasks belonging to the time system unification/refactor.")* ⭐ **The net is `Hrot.ClusterRunner.Integration.Tests` ▸ `TimeControlIntegrationTests`** — a real orchestrator + a real SimHost over `MockNetworkFactory`, a full `ClusterOpRequest` → intent → `MasterSyncController` → DDS → slave round trip. ⇒ ⭐⭐ **`T0` in `PLAN_Time_System_Refactor.md`**, and the first Batch-104 dispatch was **WITHDRAWN** under rule 1c because it began with `GlobalTime`, a kernel phase and a drain system — all time code, all before a working net | `PLAN_Time_System_Refactor.md` §1 |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-128** | ✅✅ **CONCURRENT IMPLEMENTATION LANES ARE ALLOWED, AND THREE STRUCTURAL RULES KEEP THEM APART.** ⭐⭐⭐ **The DURABLE half — the three rules:** ① **ONE ID PREFIX PER LANE** *(📌 id collisions have bitten three times — structural, not coordination)* · ② **TRACKER PARTITION** — each lane writes **only its own area**, so different regions of one file merge cleanly · ③ **NO CROSS-LANE FILES** — ⚠ a cross-lane edit is a **STOP-and-report**, never a judgement call. 📄 **Stated per-ROLE in `.claude/CLAUDE.md`'s two-session protocol.** ⭐⭐ **And the freeze MECHANISM it came from:** the user may give ONE session exclusive rights over an area while a unification lands *(because two sessions building one shared surface produce exactly two implementations)* — ⛔ **only the user declares or lifts a freeze**, it bans CODE and not design, and it is **scoped**, so *"is my work inside it?"* is a STOP-and-ask. ⛔⛔ **THE PERISHABLE HALF — the `2026-08-15` variable-model freeze, its scope, the time-lane approval and the per-lane id/area assignments — IS NOT CANON AND IS NOT REPEATED HERE.** 📌 That freeze was **LIFTED `2026-08-25`**; the dated record lives in [`PLAN_Time_System_Refactor.md`](PLAN_Time_System_Refactor.md) §5, which the probe now cites. ⇒ ⚠ **Never quote a lane assignment or a freeze scope from this row — they are STATE CLAIMS** *(see "THE LEDGER MAY NOT ASSERT WHAT THE CODE IS")* | `docs/blueprints/PLAN_Time_System_Refactor.md` |
| ⛔⛔⛔ **R-129** | ⭐⭐⭐ **READ THE OWNING DESIGN DOC BEFORE TOUCHING OR DESIGNING A CHANGE TO AN EXISTING FEATURE — the CODE is not the intent.** *(user, `2026-08-21`)* ⛔ **The full, generic rule lives in `.claude/CLAUDE.md`** *("THE INTENT IS IN THE DESIGN DOC, NOT THE CODE")* — ⭐ **put there on purpose so it is not buried among the rulings** *(user)*. ⇒ **Indexed here; read it there.** | `.claude/CLAUDE.md` |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-130** | ✅ **YELLOW = A STAGED-CHANGE INDICATOR. It is meaningless for a value written directly/immediately.** *(user, `2026-08-21`: "yellow display is an indication of staged change. makes no sense if value is directly written now. make it yellow when really staged.")* ⇒ ⭐⭐ **A row goes 🟡 yellow ONLY while a genuine STAGED write is pending** *(not yet applied by a tick)* — 📌 `DESIGN_..._Editing.md` §4a *("YOUR optimistic edit has not been applied yet — clears when the staged write lands")*. ⛔⛔ **A directly-applied write is NOT staged ⇒ NO yellow.** ⇒ ⭐⭐⭐ **CONSEQUENCE: this reaffirms the §6/`R-126` STAGED model and puts `MIN`'s direct `_liveRepo` write (`WriteFieldNow`) at odds with the design** — the honest resolution is to STAGE the write *(so the yellow is true and Flight-Recorder linearity holds — §6)*, which needs the drain-from-tick-loop *(`R-126`: PULL from the sim tick; `W1`/`W2`; `M-41` is the gap)* | `DESIGN_Variable_Details_And_Editing.md` §4a–§6 |
| ⭐⭐ **R-131** | ⛔⛔ **A CRASHING / UN-GATEABLE TEST IS A DEFECT TO RESOLVE — analyse, fix, or JUSTIFY its removal; ⛔ never a permanent filter-around.** *(user, `2026-08-23`)* 📌 `BP-378` *(`Hrot.ClusterRunner.Integration.Tests`)* + `BP-419` *(`Fdp.Presentation.Tests`)* have been gated by FILTER only for ~40 batches — that is the "generic refusal" this ends. ⭐ The subprocess harness relieves the e2e portion, but ⛔ does not excuse leaving the in-process suites broken | `DESIGN_Headless_Testability.md` |
| ⭐⭐ **R-132** | ⛔⛔ **A CURATED (HAND-AUTHORED) ARTEFACT OUTRANKS A GENERATED ONE — and two producers for one slot bound by REGISTRATION ORDER is a race, not a precedence rule.** *(user, `2026-08-23`: "if curated (hand-authored) exists, then no other is needed - having automatically generated is undesired in such a case.")* 📌 **The case:** `ApplyResolverOverlay`'s `if (def.ParseParams == null)` let a **generated** `ParseParams` win the slot, so the curated geo-aware resolver for `PlatoonHillAttack` never ran and the platoon drove to `(0,0)`. ⭐⭐ **Silent because the two resolvers expect DIFFERENT WIRE FORMATS** — the generated lambda switches on blackboard variable names, the mission plan's keys are flat and geo ⇒ every key hit a `default: break` *("unknown key: IGNORED")* and the params region stayed **zeros**: no exception, no log line. ⚠ **Found by a visual check through the MCP API; every rail was green** · ⭐ **`2026-09-27` — THE PROBE MOVED, THE RULING DID NOT** *(`CE-374`)*: the *"a human wrote this"* signal used to be *"someone typed a `RegisterResolver` call into `CgfCuratedBehaviorRegistrar`"*; that class is **deleted** and the signal is now the **`[BehaviorResolver]` attribute**, which is the same declaration in a checkable form. ⛔ Nothing about *"curated wins by declaration"* changed | `Behavior_Parameter_Resolver_Detailed_Design.md` §10 |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-134** | ⛔⛔⛔ **NETWORK IS STRICTLY SEPARATED FROM INTERNAL FDP EVENT PROCESSING — no DDS type crosses into the FDP-internal path; the egress translator is the SOLE boundary.** *(user, `2026-08-25`: the gizmo must NEVER use a DDS structure directly; the intent FDP-bus record uses its OWN enum, the egress converts to the network enum — "even at the cost of keeping the same enum duplicated in two namespaces, still numerically identical")* ⭐ **The pair already exists:** `AttributeValueKind` *(FDP-internal, `Fdp.Toolkits/Replication/Patching`)* vs `AttributeValueType` *(network, `Hrot.Network.NED`)*; precedent = `NavigationIntent` internal-vs-wire, converted only in `NavigationIntentEgressTranslator`. ⛔ **Duplication here is the CORRECT pattern, not debt.** 🔴 **As-built finding:** the merged `AttributeEntityComponentWriter` (AX-003) uses DDS `AttributeRecord`/`AttributeValueType` inside the FDP-internal path — AX-005 corrects it | `DESIGN_Cgf_AxisB_Rotation_Slice.md` §11.1 |
| ⭐⭐ **R-135** | ⭐⭐⭐ **AFTER `RULINGS.md`, READ YOUR LANE'S RESUME DOC — the auto-loaded pointer that was missing.** *(added `2026-08-27`)* 📐 **Measured:** `grep -c RESUME_UI_Lane` over `.claude/CLAUDE.md` **and** this file returned **0 and 0** ⇒ ⛔ a post-compaction session read the canon and then had to **guess** where the live work was. ⭐ **The full rule lives in `.claude/CLAUDE.md`** *("RULE ZERO-A")* — the only reliable auto-loaded channel — ⭐ **indexed here so a ledger-first reader still finds it.** ⇒ ⭐⭐ **the order is: ① this file *(canon)* → ② your lane's resume doc's `current-answer` section *(what is IN FLIGHT)* → ③ the designs it cites.** ⚠ Only the **UI lane**'s resume doc is verified to exist *(`docs/blueprints/RESUME_UI_Lane.md`)*; ⛔ do NOT assume other lanes have one | `.claude/CLAUDE.md` |
| ⭐⭐ **R-133** | ⛔⛔ **THE CAPABILITY MANIFEST IS MEASURED, NEVER DECLARED — and the known-absent BASELINE lives in the HARNESS, not in the manifest.** 📌 **The case (`2026-08-25`, cgf==editor slice 1):** the handoff and the design both said *"flip the capability-manifest cells absent→present"*, and there is **no such cell to flip** — `CapabilityManifest`'s DESCRIPTION layer is *"enumerated from the live route table"* and its AVAILABILITY layer is *"measured from what is actually wired"*. ⇒ ⭐ **a port is a reviewed DELETION from `ClusterConformanceRails.EditorOnlyKinds`**, and nothing else. ⭐⭐ **Why this is a ledger row and not a design note:** a hand-authored availability table is exactly §M's disease — it stays green while the code drifts — and this is the second time an artefact asked someone to hand-edit one. ⛔ **A cell reported present that silently no-ops is worse than an absent one** *(`AQ54` `D4`)* ⭐⭐ **CONFIRMED THE HARD WAY `2026-08-27` (`BP-487`), and the row had ONE EXCEPTION that is now closed:** this row says the availability layer is *"measured from what is actually wired"* — 📐 **it was not, for `panels.gizmo`.** `CapabilityManifest` hard-coded that cell to `true` on **every** perspective row, on the strength of a provider comment calling the primitive buffer a *"process-wide static"*. ⛔ It is one buffer **per subsystem** and ExCon has none, so `--mode all` advertised a gizmo feed that answered **404** — ⭐ precisely the *"present cell that silently no-ops"* this row calls worse than an absent one. ⇒ the cell now comes from `dispatcher.Matrix()` like every other, and `The_manifest_describes_this_host_truthfully` asserts each cell against real behaviour (with anti-vacuity, so an all-false matrix cannot pass). ⚠ **The lesson for the row: "measured" was TRUE OF THE DESIGN and FALSE OF ONE LINE** — §M applies to the manifest's own implementation, not just to hand-authored tables. ⭐⭐ **AND AGAIN, `CE-066`:** the `/missions` routes were UNCLASSIFIED for three reports and read as a paperwork gap. 📐 They were unclassified because nobody had asked what a CLUSTER host ANSWERS — *"no mission service"*, while `CgfSubsystem:1095` had built one all along. ⇒ ⭐⭐⭐ **an unclassified route is a MEASUREMENT that was never taken, not a form that was never filled.** ⛔ `mission.edit` is now its own key, measured per provider — ⚠ deliberately NOT folded into the broad `EditorAuthoring` cell, which would have made it undiagnosable in exactly the way this row warns about. | `Hrot/Subsystems/Hrot.Editor/DebugApi/CapabilityManifest.cs` |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-136** | 🔒🔒🔒 **ENTITY STATE HAS EXACTLY TWO LEGAL SOURCES: the TKB, or a published `TransientLocal` descriptor. ANYTHING IN NEITHER MUST NOT EXIST AS DURABLE STATE.** 📄 **[`DESIGN_Entity_State_Sourcing.md`](../DESIGN_Entity_State_Sourcing.md)** *(the intent)* · **[`Architect_Question_64`](Architect_Question_64_Scenario_Component_Overrides_Across_The_Wire.md)** *(the archaeology — four rejected designs)*. ⭐⭐ **Why it is an ENGINE INVARIANT and not a preference:** NED's contract is that a late joiner reconstructs every entity **purely by listening to DDS**. The TKB is a static per-node asset held offline *(never on DDS)* and descriptor history is `Reliable`+`TransientLocal`, so the two together are **complete by construction** ⇒ ⛔ a THIRD source is not "less elegant", it is **UNRECONSTRUCTIBLE**, and it makes two nodes' view of one entity diverge **permanently with nothing to detect it**. 📐 **The corollary that does the work, measured `2026-08-28`: STATE is `TransientLocal` and COMMANDS are `Volatile`** *(`GenericDescriptors.cs:77/134/168` + `MapDescriptors.cs`×6 vs `GenericMessages.cs`)* ⇒ ⛔ **a command can never carry anything durable.** 📌 **The case that proved it:** a wait-FLAG in `EntityMaster` plus a one-shot bundle looked feasible and is **WORSE THAN NOTHING** — a late joiner reads the flag out of TransientLocal history and waits forever for a `Volatile` sample that is long gone, so **the ghost never promotes**. ⇒ ⭐⭐ **every "flag + side-channel" scheme has this shape: the flag is durable, the channel is not.** ⭐ **The test, per component: "does this state need to survive a late join?" YES ⇒ published descriptor · NO ⇒ TKB. There is no third answer.** ⚠ **Two filters that look right and are not:** *"is it registered on the consuming node?"* *(📐 excludes 1 of 23 — useless)* and *"is it written by a TKB translator?"* *(better, but describes today's wiring, not the requirement)*. ⛔ **And the boundary: a runtime parameter COMMAND has the same hole moved** — safe only as a transient/authoring action with divergence knowingly accepted, ⛔ **never as the general override mechanism**; durable difference from the TKB requires RECLASSIFICATION into a published descriptor. | `docs/DESIGN_Entity_State_Sourcing.md` |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-140** | 🔒🔒 **NODE ROLES CARRY A PERSISTENCE CONVENTION: IG IS A PASSIVE, NON-PERSISTING NODE AND MAY NOT OWN PERSISTABLE ENTITIES.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-02`, verbatim:** *"by convention it is considered passive listening node, not maintaining any persistent state. If IG creates entities, then only temporary ones, possibly shared with other IGs, but never persisted to scenario. If IG crashes, its entities are gone, but no one cares, they were temporary anyway. There can be many IGs in the system, dynamically added and removed, none should affect the scenario being edited. This was the reason why persistable entities can not be owned by IGs and why the request was sent to another node who owns them and who saves them to scenario because he is allowed to save."* ⭐⭐ **This is the missing half of `R-138`** — that ruling says every node CAN create and that `NodeRole` is a convention giving *"some ownership expectations"*; ⇒ **this states what IG's expectation IS.** ⛔ **Not a protocol restriction** — nothing prevents IG owning anything; it is a rule about what SHOULD be owned where. ⇒ ⭐⭐ **entity creation therefore has TWO shapes and the request must carry a TARGET OWNER**: an IG sketch is IG-owned and temporary; a scenario tactical graphic is REQUESTED from a saving node which owns and persists it. ⭐ `EntityCreationRequest.OwnerAppInstanceId` is that choice and the level-1 routing guard already honours it. ⚠⚠ **ENFORCEMENT GAP, measured `2026-09-02`:** `StagingEntityExtractor.BuildStaticMask()` **STRIPS** `NetworkOwnership`/`NetworkAuthority`/`NetworkIdentity`/`DescriptorOwnership`/`TkbIdentity`/`GhostStateTracker`/`PendingNetworkAck` from the save — ⇒ **ownership is DISCARDED at save time, never consulted**, so nothing can distinguish an IG-owned entity and this rule is a CONVENTION ONLY. ⭐ Good half: owner choice does NOT silently change persistence, so the two axes are independent. 🔴 Bad half: **if an IG-owned entity ever reaches the extracted world it WOULD be saved.** ⛔ **NOT MEASURED — settle before host (f):** whether IG-owned entities replicate into that world at all *(the extractor lives in `Hrot.CGF`, i.e. runs on the Brain)*. If they do, the rule needs a MECHANISM, not a convention.  📄 **FULL TREATMENT: [`docs/DESIGN_Node_Roles_And_Policies.md`](../DESIGN_Node_Roles_And_Policies.md)** — ⛔ this row is the INDEX, not the explanation. | `docs/HROT-Engine-Guide/HROT-Engine-Guide.md` §1.3c |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-138** | 🔒🔒🔒 **THE SYSTEM IS FULLY DISTRIBUTED: EVERY ECS NODE CAN CREATE ENTITIES, AND OWNERSHIP IS PER-COMPONENT, DYNAMIC AND TRANSFERABLE. `NodeRole` IS A CONVENTION, NEVER A PROTOCOL RESTRICTION.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-01`, verbatim:** *"each host can spawn entitites on its own"* … *"the ECS component ownership is trasferrable per entity during entity lifetime, and some other node can create its own entities and keep the SimTransform ownership for them. System should be fully distributed and nodes should be equal, and their role (by convention) gives them some ownership expectations."* ⭐⭐ **The two false principles this retires, both of which were LIVE TEXT in the corpus:** ⛔ ① *"CGF holds spawn authority / exactly one node may spawn"* — 📐 **true only of UNOWNED BROADCAST requests** (`OwnerAppInstanceId == 0`, e.g. from ExCon), where one `isDefaultProcessor`/`isBroadcastArbiter` node services it so the entity is not created twice. ⭐ `CreateEntityRequestSystem`'s level-1 guard processes a request targeted at the local node **unconditionally, ignoring `isDefaultProcessor`**, and IDs come from the **distributed** `DdsIdAllocatorServer` ⇒ **peer-to-peer genesis is the architecture, not a future goal.** ⛔ ② *"SimHost owns `SimTransform`"* — 📐 that is the **OUTCOME** of CGF's `BrainMuscleOwnershipStrategy` delegating kinematics for entities **CGF spawned** (`DeferredTakeOwnership` → `DeferredTakeoverSystem`), ⛔ **not a property of SimHost.** ⭐ A node that originates its own entity **keeps what it creates** and delegates nothing; and authority moves at runtime over the **`OwnershipUpdate`** topic (`OwnershipIngressSystem` → `SetAuthority(entity, componentId, isAuth)`), with the previous owner symmetrically yielding. ⇒ ⭐⭐⭐ **THE CHECKABLE FORM: never write a design sentence of the shape *"node X owns Y"* or *"only X can Z"*. Write *"X owns Y **for entities it originated / was delegated**"*, or name the CONFIGURATION FLAG that makes it so.** ⚠ **Why this is canon and not a design note:** the wrong version was in the **top-level orientation doc** (`HROT-Engine-Guide` §1.3/§1.5/§11.3), both `SOLUTION-OVERVIEW`s, the pipeline reference **and** `DESIGN_Entity_Creation_Unification` §2.2 — ⛔ where it sat as live text **directly above the §2.3 that retracts it**. ⇒ a session reading any one of them reasons off a centralised model the code has never had. 📌 **All corrected `2026-09-01`; `HROT-Engine-Guide` §1.3a now carries the positive statement.**  📄 **FULL TREATMENT: [`docs/DESIGN_Node_Roles_And_Policies.md`](../DESIGN_Node_Roles_And_Policies.md)** — ⛔ this row is the INDEX, not the explanation. | `docs/HROT-Engine-Guide/HROT-Engine-Guide.md` §1.3a |

| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-141** | 🔒🔒 **AN UNUSED CAPABILITY IS THE NATURAL OUTCOME OF SHARING — ⛔ NEVER A PER-HOST DECISION TO JUSTIFY.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-03`, verbatim:** *"this should be the natural outcome of sharing the code, not a per-host decision; unused capability does not harm is present as sharing/unification is more important for overall maintenance"* ⭐⭐ **The question this RETIRES:** *"does host X actually need this affordance before we give it one?"* ⛔ **Do not ask it.** 📌 The case: I proposed giving `SimHost` and `ReplayBrowser` the shared authoring adapter and **flagged it for approval as capability they never asked for** — ⭐ the user ruled that framing itself wrong. ⇒ ⭐⭐⭐ **When a capability arrives on a host purely because that host adopted shared code, its unusedness is NOT a cost to weigh against the sharing.** ⚠ **This is NOT a licence to build speculative features** — it governs the *distribution* of something already being built, not the *decision to build it*; the round-out and demand-driven rules in `CLAUDE.md` are untouched. ⭐ **The checkable form: a design that gives host X a shared affordance owes NO per-host justification, and a reviewer must not ask for one.** ⛔ Conversely a design that WITHHOLDS a shared affordance from one adopting host owes an explicit reason. 📄 **FULL TREATMENT: [`docs/DESIGN_Entity_Authoring_Surface.md`](../DESIGN_Entity_Authoring_Surface.md) §5b.2.** | `docs/DESIGN_Entity_Authoring_Surface.md` §5b.2 |

| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-142** | ⛔⛔⛔ **EVERY FEATURE HAS ITS OWN RAILS — FIND AND RUN THEM FIRST; ADD ONLY IF NECESSARY, AND INTO THAT SUITE.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-04`, verbatim:** *"remember that every feature has own rails so first thing to do to verify if feature still works is to find and run those and add others only if necessary and to the feature's suite preferrably."* ⭐⭐ **The full rule lives in `.claude/CLAUDE.md`** *("T-1 — EVERY FEATURE HAS ITS OWN RAILS")* — the only reliable auto-loaded channel; ⭐ indexed here so a ledger-first reader still finds it. ⭐⭐⭐ **It comes BEFORE `T0` and applies to ANY question of the form *"does X still work?"*** — touching X, debugging X, or merely reasoning about X. 📌 **The miss that produced it:** while fixing the ownership handover I wrote a NEW rail class when **`SplitAuthoritySpawnTests` (`IT-SA-1..3`) — that exact feature's suite — already existed.** ⭐ The sequence: ① FIND the suite *(`scripts/find.sh <FeatureName>`; do NOT assume there is none)* → ② RUN it, **that is the verification** → ③ ⚠ **if it is GREEN while the feature is BROKEN, THAT is the finding — fix the blindness IN PLACE, do not route around it** *(measured: SPLIT-AUTH-IT never set `FdpConfig.EnforceExplicitEventRegistration`, the guard `Program.cs:52` sets in production, so the throw was a silent no-op in tests)* → ④ ADD only if necessary and **into the feature's suite**, preferring to fold the assertion into an existing test that already builds the fixture. 📐 **Adding a 4th test to `SplitAuthoritySpawnTests` deterministically reddened `IT-SA-3` across 3 runs** by adding a 4th DDS domain ⇒ **a new test in that class is not free.** ⛔ **A new test class for a feature that HAS a suite is BLOAT and a review finding.** ⭐ **The checkable artefact: the report NAMES the feature's suite and its result before it names any new rail.** | `.claude/CLAUDE.md` |

| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-143** | 🔒🔒🔒 **THE WHOLE SIMULATION IS DRIVEN BY SIM TIME ONLY. THE ONLY LEGITIMATE USE OF THE WALL CLOCK IS STAMPING THE FDP RECORDING.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-08`, verbatim:** *"no wall clock enywhere, whole sim driven by sim time ONLY. only use of wallclock us stamping the fdp recording"* — stated while correcting *"our animation is driven through our own bridge on wallDt"*. ⭐⭐ **This is broader than physics**, which is where it was first applied *(`CE-227`: `GameTime.Factor = simDelta / wallDelta`, so Stride's own integrator consumes sim seconds)*. ⇒ **animation, view interpolation, gizmo buffers and every host tick are in scope too.** ⛔⛔ **It RETIRES the "the view tier is free-running, so `wallDt` is correct here" reading** recorded in `DESIGN_Stride_Node_Modes.md` §7.3b — that cell is corrected there. ⭐ **Why it is canon and not a preference:** a node whose visuals or animation advance on frame time diverges from a node that renders at a different rate, and the divergence is invisible until two nodes are compared — the same class of defect as a wall-dt physics step, one tier up. ⚠ **What is NOT in scope:** diagnostics and log throttles *(`Stopwatch` for "warn at most once a second")*, camera/input controllers *(operator interaction, not simulation)*, and the FDP recording's own timestamps. ⭐ **The checkable form: a `dt` that reaches ANY simulation, animation or presentation-of-simulation code must originate from the synced clock** — ⛔ never from `GameTime.Elapsed`. | `.claude/CLAUDE.md` |

| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-144** | ✅✅ **ONE GIZMO FOCUS REGISTRY — AND "THE FOCUS HOLDER RECEIVES UN-ANCHORED INPUT" IS THE PRIMARY ROUTING RULE, NOT A FALLBACK.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-09`:** *"68 most recent leans approved"* ⇒ all four of `Q68`. ⭐⭐ **The decisions:** ① **one registry** — `GizmoFocusRegistry` in `Fdp.Toolkits`, both arbiters delegating *(⛔ **not** a port of `GizmoInteractionManager`: that would fuse a focus fix with a keying fix whose cross-node blast radius is unmeasured)* · ② the un-anchored-input rule is **KEPT**, sourced from the registry and stated once as **`RecipientFor(...)`** — ⚠ **the focus holder FIRST, a target lookup only as the second arm** *(`_focusedGizmo ?? FindGizmo(...)`; writing that order backwards silently re-routes every drag/commit/cancel, and a first draft of this row did)*. ⭐⭐⭐ **Why the name matters and this is not cosmetic:** `DataDrivenGizmoSystem`'s `_focusedGizmo ?? FindGizmo(...)` reads as a defensive fallback for an unresolvable token, and that misreading is what made it look undesigned. 📐 **Measured: `GlobalGizmoManager.Execute` NEVER READS `evt.Token`** — it delivers every raw mouse/key event to the focus holder unconditionally ⇒ **the two arbiters run the SAME policy**, one with an extra entity-scoped arm. 📐 **And `MakeInputCaptureBinding` never stamps a `GizmoTypeId`** *(`DebugPrimitive.cs:344-350`)*, so raw-input tokens carry `GizmoTypeId == 0` and `FindGizmo` can essentially never match ⇒ ⛔⛔ **deleting the "fallback" would not lose an edge case, it would delete raw mouse/keyboard input for entity-scoped gizmos outright.** ⚠ **What approval does NOT cover:** the keying violation *(`AnchorId` documented network-stable, stamped with an ECS `Entity.Index` — `CE-259h`, unresolved; the registry is deliberately keying-agnostic)*, IG's `_activeSequenceGizmo` *(fire-and-forget, needs `Activate` not `PushModal`)*, and ⛔ **the SPATIAL routing path, which was never compared between the two arbiters** — only the raw-input path was. 📄 **[`Architect_Question_68`](Architect_Question_68_Gizmo_Focus_Registry.md) §6** is the ruling; §4c/§4d are the measurements | `Architect_Question_68_Gizmo_Focus_Registry.md` §6 |

| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-145** | ✅✅ **AUTHOR vs TRANSLATOR — A NEW INTENT MUST GO THROUGH THE AUTHORING AFFORDANCE; AN EXISTING REPRESENTATION BEING MAPPED IN MUST NOT.** ⭐⭐ **The rule, in one line:** an **AUTHOR** *(a human gesture, an AI decision, a tool — nothing outside has decided the fields yet)* calls `EntityCreation.RequestEntityCreation`; a **TRANSLATOR** *(a scenario file, a DDS sample, another node's message — the representation already fixed owner, components and id)* constructs `EntityCreationRequest` directly, ⛔ **and that is CORRECT, not a loophole.** 📐 **Why the exemption is principled and not convenience:** an affordance exists to make the *authoring choice* explicit — *"do I own this, or does the arbiter?"* — and a translator **has no such choice**; routing it through would add a defaulting layer over fields already fully determined, and the two production translators *(`StagingEntityExtractor`, `NedCgfEntityLifecycleAdapters`)* set `PreAllocatedNetworkId` / `ChildComponentOverrides`, which the affordance deliberately excludes. ⚠⚠ **STATED AS THE WEAKER GUARANTEE IT IS:** the original argument was *"a translator CANNOT express its case through the affordance"* — ⛔ **that died with the two-method shape**; `owner` now takes any node id, so a translator *could* call it. ⇒ **the exemption is by DEFINITION, not by the type system**, and the check is a reviewer reading §2's table, not a compiler. ⛔⛔ **AND THE TRIGGER IS NOT *"am I creating an entity"*** — it is 🔒 ***"does this entity need a NETWORK IDENTITY?"***: local probe entities *(the AI's EQS sensor children)* go straight through `ctx.World` and must **NOT** come through the affordance, which would hand them a network id and a lifecycle handshake they have no use for. 📄 **FULL TREATMENT: [`docs/DESIGN_Entity_Authoring_Surface.md`](../DESIGN_Entity_Authoring_Surface.md) §2, §7 R1, §7b** — resolved BY REASONING per the user's *"the questions you are asking should be answered by your reasoning, not my ruling"* *(`2026-09-03`)*, and written into the code `2026-09-12` | `docs/DESIGN_Entity_Authoring_Surface.md` §2 |
| ⭐⭐ **R-147** | ✅ **THE NAS IS THE MASTER, A NODE HOLDS WHAT IT WILL LOAD — AND THE "NEEDS" VOCABULARY IS AN ADAPTER OVER THE LOAD TABLE, NEVER A SECOND TABLE.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-19`:** *"ok accepting your lean"*, on the last open row of `AQ-72`. ⭐⭐ **The decision:** `hrot.asset.needs.*` is derived from `RoleLoadRequirements` through a **3-row `LoadPart → AssetKind` adapter** owned by `DESIGN_Asset_Management` §7.3a, **plus ONE rule — `Brain ⇒ all AI asset kinds`**. 🔒 *"an adapter between two EXISTING vocabularies is not a third vocabulary"*; the brain rule is **one line, not a table**, so there is nothing to maintain per kind. ⛔⛔ **`RoleLoadRequirements` is NOT extended** — 📐 it is an ordered execution list with one `ILoadPartProvider` per part (`LoadPhaseChain.cs:97`), so a `LoadPart.BehaviorAssets` would make the table **claim a load step nobody implements** *(`R-133`)*, and it is owned by `DESIGN_Node_Roles_And_Policies` §3.2. 📐 **The gap that forced it:** `LoadPart` = {KnowledgeBase, Terrain, ScenarioEntities} and `AssetKind` = {Blueprint, BTree, Hsm, Blackboard, Utility, Scenario} — **the intersection is EMPTY**, and behaviour assets have no `LoadPart` at all. ⇒ ⭐ **`Q72-L`'s derivation stands, NARROWED**: the *needs* come from the load table, the *kinds* do not fall out of it. ⛔⛔ **AND THE RULE IS UNSAFE WITHOUT ITS SUBTRACTION — `Q72-M` amendment ①, `2026-09-19`: A NODE IS NEVER AN AUTOMATIC SYNC TARGET FOR A KIND IT AUTHORS.** 📐 *"Brain host"* and *"authoring host"* are **the same hosts by default** — `CgfSubsystem.DefaultRole = NodeRole.Brain` (`:92`), editor likewise (`EditorSubsystem.cs:1451`), and **both read AI assets from the AUTHORING root** (`CgfSubsystem.cs:2078/2506`) ⇒ an unsubtracted mirror lands **on the folder the author is editing** and **destroys unpublished work**, while the probe warns about the very changes it just erased. ⭐ Subtract `hrot.asset.authors.<kind>` — ⛔⛔ **in THREE clauses, because the bare subtraction is BOTH VACUOUS AND OVER-BROAD** *(round-3 review)*: ① subtract · ② ⭐⭐ **the token is CONFIGURED, never host-typed** — 🔴 **there is NO Brain host today that is not an authoring host** *(`SimHostApp.DefaultRole` = `MuscleGround|Perception|NavigationSolver`, `:182-183`; `SimHostNodeBootstrapper.cs:415` reads `Brain` as "this run is the CGF")*, so a host-typed token subtracts from **all of them** and the feature reaches **nobody** · ③ ⭐⭐⭐ **for an authored kind the sync is ADD-ONLY, never overwrite/delete** — which makes a misconfiguration cost **staleness (reported)** instead of **lost work (silent)**. ⛔ **BOUNDED: never a `LoadPart`-derived kind** — the editor authors SCENARIOS (`EditorSubsystem.cs:3915`) and is `Brain`, so unbounded it subtracts `LoadPart.ScenarioEntities` from itself and `ScenarioLoadStep.cs:122` throws. 🔒 **No authorship claim may override a load requirement.** ⚠ **Consequence, stated:** an authoring host never AUTOMATICALLY receives an UPDATE to a file it holds ⇒ report **BEHIND as well as AHEAD** in the probe *(warn, overwrite nothing)*; ✅ **the explicit refresh IS RULED** *(user, `2026-09-19`: "w6 — yes, seems useful")* ⇒ **three operations close the loop — PUBLISH (user) · SYNC (load, add-only) · REFRESH (user)**, the last being the ONLY route by which an update reaches a file an author already holds; ⛔ it overwrites the author's own folder, so it warns with the file list and is **never** automatic. ⛔ **Amendment ②: the AI-kind set is a PREDICATE, not a list** — `AssetRoots.AssetsRelative:197-204` **throws** for `Blackboard`/`Utility`/`Scenario` ⇒ *"syncable iff the contributor exposes a non-null `BaseFolder`"*. ⚠ **What would widen it:** a behaviour asset only SOME brain hosts need, or a standby node needing bytes it does not load — **both are widenings of the adapter, not redesigns.** 📄 **[`Architect_Question_72`](Architect_Question_72_Asset_Lifecycle_And_Distribution.md) §Q72-M** is the ruling; `DESIGN_Asset_Management.md` §7.3a carries the map and §7.3b the subtraction | `docs/blueprints/Architect_Question_72_Asset_Lifecycle_And_Distribution.md` §Q72-M |
| ⭐⭐ **R-148** | ✅ **LANES RUN ON STABLE ROLE-NAMED BRANCHES; ANCESTRY STILL VERIFIES.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-19`:** *"the web harness adds a postfix but the session can be switched to any concrete branch"* ⇒ the lanes run on role names — **`coordinator` · `behaviors` · `backend` · `ui`** *(windows is not a branch)* — replacing the random-postfixed defaults that had `session-design-brief.sh` rewritten twice as they went stale. ⛔ ~~All derive from the `coordinator` trunk; the brief gate keys on the name `coordinator`~~ — ⭐⭐ **AMENDED `2026-10-02` (user): lanes are PEERS** — *"every lane can be a coordinator"* · *"currently no dedicated integration trunk"* ⇒ each lane merges the lane(s) it builds on; tracker ids come from a **per-lane `CE-` block** *(table in the tracker header — "renumbering is bad as number often gets written to sources as comments")*; the DESIGN BRIEF is **per lane** on every branch. ⛔ **NOT a return to "trust a name":** `git merge-base` still verifies what a commit descends from — the stable name is only where a lane RUNS. ⚠ `behaviors` = BTree/HSM/Blueprint + behavior infrastructure; `ui` touches any UI surface, not just CGF. 📄 `.claude/CLAUDE.md` §"Lane protocol". | `.claude/CLAUDE.md` |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-146** | 🔒🔒 **A ZONE IS A GEOGRAPHIC WINDOW ONTO A TERRAIN — NEVER A CONTENT BUNDLE. TERRAIN IS THE CONTENT PACK AND IS GLOBAL; ROADS AND OBSTACLES ARE WORLD CONTENT AUTHORED AS ENTITIES.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-16`:** *"terrain… is the superset of zones. Terrain is the whole, zone is a subpart of it… Zone is always created for a concrete terrain database, but terrain db id is not a property of zone, it is **part of zone's identity**"* — and, retracting an earlier ruling of their own, *"'zones should for sure be handled by brain' — i was likely wrong… they belong to the muscle/navig/perception role."* ⭐⭐ **The three axes, which `packs-3` fused and this separates:** ① **terrain** = global, already `GlobalContext.SceneId` *("the map/terrain identifier")* · ② **zone** = `ZoneSpec {ZoneId, Bounds, DataPath}`, a staged-load window *(`mgmt-1` §11)* · ③ **roads/obstacles** = world content crossing zone boundaries freely, authored as ENTITIES and compiled by an explicit build. 📐 **Measured:** `TerrainDatabaseId` had **zero production readers**; zone obstacles were **double-represented** *(the manifest re-creates them AND the save gate persists them as entities)*; the merge's *"Zones are brain-only"* throw cited a rule the wiring never followed *(the **muscle** is the wired source)*; and `packs-3/DESIGN.md` §2.B **contradicts its own design conversation** *(`.dev/_DONE/packs-3/design_talk.md:555-567` argued for a zone-id foreign key and against a new scenario section)*. ⇒ ⭐ **the entity→asset step is an explicit BUILD** — `PrepareTerrainAsset`/`CommitTerrainAsset`, a kind-masked 2PC round riding the existing barrier, prepare converting into staged memory and commit swapping pointers. ⚠ **Approved FAKE-FIRST:** authoring + referencing + the op rounds are REAL; what an asset IS and what the swap replaces are **POSTPONED**. ⛔ **A fake handler MUST announce itself** *(`R-133`: a capability reported present that silently no-ops is worse than an absent one)*. 📄 **[`Architect_Question_71`](Architect_Question_71_Terrain_Zones_And_The_Asset_Build.md) §5** is the ruling; §6 is the gap list. ⛔ This row is the INDEX, not the explanation. | `docs/blueprints/Architect_Question_71_Terrain_Zones_And_The_Asset_Build.md` |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-149** | ✅ **A PARAMS REGION *NAMES* ITS RESOLVER — SO TWO RESOLVERS FOR ONE REGION ARE UNREPRESENTABLE, NOT ARBITRATED.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-21`:** *"blueprint graph defined resolver will likely not compete with hand written, how could it? resolver is per graph and someone needs to name the manually coded resolver somehow — how in a blueprint? maybe the property of the blueprint itself (UI picked), and if defined, then it is clear that this blueprint will not generate own resolver."* ⭐⭐ **The decision:** a params region names **ONE** resolver, so the collision has nothing to arbitrate. ⛔⛔ **The three-arm `ParamResolverRef` property this row first described as THE MECHANISM is SUPERSEDED `2026-09-21` — do NOT quote it; §M applies, the RULING is canon and the mechanism was a state claim.** 📐 Re-measured after `E8a`: the **blueprint** half needs no property *(one key, no binding step — `CSharpEmitter.cs:456`, and `BP1676` already forbids a second own-resolver)*, and the other two arms only ever resolve to a **BEHAVIOUR** *(a reusable resolver is typed on an AUTHORED DTO while a blueprint's `Params` is GENERATED — `AiPrimitiveEmitter.cs:151`; and `BehaviorRegistry.cs:468` keys on BEHAVIOUR names)*. ⇒ ⭐ the property lives on a **blackboard params VARIABLE** — 📄 **[`DESIGN_Per_Variable_Param_Resolver.md`](DESIGN_Per_Variable_Param_Resolver.md)**; the retired shape and its measurements are in `DESIGN_Resolver_World_Reach.md` §7.2a. ⇒ ⛔ **no precedence rule, no registration-order race** — the collision is made unrepresentable rather than arbitrated, the move `LibraryEmitter` already states for its `__loc_` prefix. ⭐⭐⭐ **The ROLE is assigned by the PROPERTY, not the graph KIND**, which is what honours `Q43-A2′`'s *"do not define `Construction` as the resolver graph"*: an Instance asset may later carry a second `Construction` graph meaning *"configure the instance"* with no ambiguity. ⭐ **Granularity — ONE RESOLVER PER PARAMS REGION**, and *"variable"* is simply what a region is CALLED on a behaviour blackboard: 📐 measured over the 30 shipped behaviour assets, a params variable IS one hosted primitive's params and the corpus names it so *(`bpParamsCalculateSegments`, `bpParamsA`, `bpParamsSharedA`)*; ⛔⛔ but site→variable is **MANY-TO-ONE** *(`PlatoonHillAttack` has 7 `ExpressionTargetField` bindings all naming `Params`)*, so per-SITE would resolve the same bytes 7× where per-VARIABLE resolves once — which is also why `Q43-B` refused a per-site resolver. ⚠ Offered only where a params region exists, ⛔ never on `Role=State` working state. ⛔⛔ **SUPERSEDES my own "rank by AUTHORSHIP" answer of the same day**, which arbitrated a competition this model prevents; `R-132` is untouched and still governs its own case *(a curated C# overlay vs the BTree generator's incidental `ParseParams`)*. 📄 **[`DESIGN_Resolver_World_Reach.md`](DESIGN_Resolver_World_Reach.md) §7.2** | `docs/blueprints/DESIGN_Resolver_World_Reach.md` §7.2 |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-150** | 🔒🔒 **COMPLEXITY THAT AN AUTHOR MUST UNDERSTAND IS A COST, AND AN UNADOPTED CAPABILITY IS NOT A NEUTRAL ONE.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-28`, verbatim:** *"whatever more complex will anyway not survive the contact with behavior author's reality - too many options and complxity = not adopted"* ⭐⭐ **The checkable form:** when a mechanism offers the author N options, a design owes the MEASURED adoption of each — ⛔ not an argument that each is useful in principle. 📌 **The case that produced it:** `WorkingStateScope {Node, Behavior, Entity}`. Measured over the whole corpus — `Node` *(the DEFAULT)* had **0** declared users and silently provisioned nothing; `Entity` was reachable only as an implicit default nobody chose; the shared-memory accessor built on it had **1** cross-entity consumer, its own proof asset. ⇒ three author-facing options, one of which worked as documented. ⭐⭐⭐ **This does NOT override `R-137`** *(a unification may not silently cost a capability)* — the two compose: `R-137` says do not DROP a capability while merging code; `R-150` says do not COUNT an unadopted option as a benefit when weighing whether to keep it. ⛔ A removal still needs the user's ruling; ⭐ what changes is that *"someone might want it"* is no longer evidence. ⚠ **Nor is it a licence to delete what is merely unreferenced** — `R-129` still binds: `PlatoonHillAttack2` is used by **zero** production behaviours and **16** proof suites, and is KEPT for exactly that reason. 📄 [`Architect_Question_76`](Architect_Question_76_One_Blackboard_Block_Per_Primitive.md) §1.4, §6 | `docs/blueprints/Architect_Question_76_One_Blackboard_Block_Per_Primitive.md` |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-151** | ⚠ **Vocabulary `2026-10-13` (`CE-3193`): read `Role=Input` as the DTO in reserved `Params`, `Role=State` as working state (derived from `WorkingStateTargetField`) — the roles are deleted, the ruling stands.** ✅✅ **ONE BLACKBOARD BLOCK PER RUNNING BEHAVIOUR — AND THE BRAIN STATE IS NOT PART OF IT.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-29`, verbatim:** *"whatever leads to this single-blackboard-slot-per-running-behavior is authorized"* · *"brain state was never part of the blackboard so it is ok to be in another slot"*. ⭐⭐ **The model:** a running behaviour — root or hosted child — owns ONE contiguous block holding its `Role=Input` variables and its `Role=State` variables together. The brain state (a BTree cursor, an HSM instance) stays in its own slot: it is not blackboard, and an HSM instance's width is chosen at RUNTIME from the blob, so it cannot be a field of a compile-time struct. ⭐⭐⭐ **The four requirements that came with the approval, and they are part of the ruling:** ① editor-saved defaults are BAKED into the whole block, not just the params · ② the behaviour parameters OVERLAY them — for a sub-behaviour those parameters are a variable of the HOST's block · ③ a custom resolver may write the WHOLE block; the DEFAULT resolver just copies the parameters into the `Role=Input` part · ④ with a custom resolver a behaviour need declare NO `Role=Input` variable at all. ⭐ A custom resolver may be hand-written C# or a blueprint `Construction` graph. ⛔⛔ **What this does NOT authorise:** `IHostVariableAccess.TryWrite` — a resolver writing its HOST's variables. The grant is write access to a resolver's OWN block; `Q76`-`D` stays open and its seam header still forbids the write path. 📄 [`Architect_Question_76`](Architect_Question_76_One_Blackboard_Block_Per_Primitive.md) §4-`B`, §12 | `docs/blueprints/Architect_Question_76_One_Blackboard_Block_Per_Primitive.md` |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-152** | ✅✅ **ONE RESOLVER PER BEHAVIOUR, NOT PER VARIABLE — AND THE RESOLVER'S JOB IS CONVERSION.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-28`:** *"the resolver is one and fills whatever blackboard variable needs to be filled. we can hardly have multiple construction scripts per blueprint so one per variable seems impossible."* 🔒 **User, `2026-09-29`:** *"We already decided that per variable resolver is NOT wanted nor possible. Resolves does conversion if needed."* ⇒ ⛔ **`DESIGN_Per_Variable_Param_Resolver.md` (`E8c`) is WITHDRAWN**, `CE-419` is RESOLVED and `CE-426` is unblocked. ⭐⭐ **Why it costs nothing:** `E8c`'s motivating measurement was *"a behaviour with one geo variable must hand-write the parse for ALL of them"* — true only because a curated resolver today REPLACES the whole `__parseParams`. `Q76` §12.3 changes exactly that: bake and overlay stay **generated for every variable, always**, and the resolver is a **third stage that refines the block IN PLACE** ⇒ the geo variable is fixed alone. **The granularity `E8c` wanted was a workaround for a pipeline shape the new design removes.** ⚠ **SHARPENS `R-149`, does not replace it:** `R-149`'s *"a params region names its resolver"* stands; this fixes what *region* means — **the behaviour's whole blackboard, never a single variable**. 📌 `E8c`'s own `D2` leaned the same way from the other end. ⛔⛔ **RECORDED LATE, and it cost a round trip:** decided `2026-09-28` in conversation and written nowhere — grepped `2026-09-29`, neither quote appeared under `docs/` — so `CE-419` read as an open three-way for a day and the user answered the same question twice. **`RULE ZERO` obligation 2, missed on a ruling already given.** 📄 [`Architect_Question_76`](Architect_Question_76_One_Blackboard_Block_Per_Primitive.md) §12.11 | `docs/blueprints/Architect_Question_76_One_Blackboard_Block_Per_Primitive.md` |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-153** | ✅✅ **A RE-ASSIGN IS NOT SPECIAL — EVERY ASSIGN STARTS THE WHOLE BLOCK FROM EMPTY: clear → bake defaults → overlay JSON → resolve.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-28`:** *"why would re-assigning the same behaviour deserve special handling, this happens rarely (certainly not every tick or two)."* ⇒ ⛔ **no carry-over of the previous root slot's bytes**, neither across a behaviour change nor on a same-behaviour re-assign; an unmentioned variable lands on its **authored default**. ⭐ A caller that wants to change one parameter re-sends the whole set — assignment is not a per-tick event. ⛔⛔ **RECORDED LATE, AND IT COST A WRONG BUILD:** the ruling lived only in tracker row `CE-421`; `CE-437` (same programme, next day) then built exactly the same-behaviour gate it rejects, and `CE-426` reverted it. **`RULE ZERO` obligation 2, missed.** 📐 Measured before building: every production `AssignBehaviorEvent` publisher sends a complete set. 📄 [`Architect_Question_76`](Architect_Question_76_One_Blackboard_Block_Per_Primitive.md) §12.18a, `CE-421` ⚠⚠ **AMENDED `2026-09-30` by `R-155`:** the pipeline is now *clear → bake defaults → **either** the default copy **or** the resolver (which receives the SOURCE)* — the resolver REPLACES the overlay, it does not run after it. The no-carry-over rule stands. | `docs/blueprints/Architect_Question_76_One_Blackboard_Block_Per_Primitive.md` |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-154** | ✅✅ **RESOLVERS ARE LOOKED UP BY BEHAVIOUR NAME; ONLY HOSTED BLUEPRINT PRIMITIVES ARE LOOKED UP BY ASSET ID.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-29`:** *"hand written behaviors have no assets, they are just code."* ⇒ ⛔ **never key a behaviour-level resolver (root or hosted subtree) on an asset GUID, and never invent a synthetic asset id for a hand-written behaviour.** ⭐ `BehaviorRegistry.RegisterResolver(name, …)` is the behaviour table (`R-132`: curated wins by declaration); `HostedParamResolvers.Register(assetId, …)` is the AiPrimitive-blueprint table — two keys for two different subjects, not one concept twice. 📐 Measured: 3 of 5 curated resolvers (`MoveToLocation`, `FireAtTarget`, `FollowRoute`) belong to hand-written behaviours with no asset. ⚠ Overturns `CE-427`'s filed wording *"the run-time lookup unifies on asset id"*. 📄 [`Architect_Question_76`](Architect_Question_76_One_Blackboard_Block_Per_Primitive.md) §12.19, `CE-427` ⚠⚠ **AMENDED `2026-09-30` by `R-155`:** actions / conditions / activities / guards have **no resolver at all** ⇒ the asset-id table (`HostedParamResolvers`) is to be retired (`CE-445`); *by behaviour name* is then the only key. | `docs/blueprints/Architect_Question_76_One_Blackboard_Block_Per_Primitive.md` |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-155** | ✅✅ **BEHAVIOURS TAKE INPUTS ONCE, AT START; ACTIONS / CONDITIONS READ THE HOST LIVE, WITH NO COPY AND NO RESOLVER.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-30`, verbatim:** *"actions are not behaviors, so they reference host blackboard (no param copy, they do not have any resolver) and respond to changes in host blackoard immediately even mid run. Hsm is a behavior, so it should follow the "my model" above, taking inputs only when they start."* · *"only behaviors have optional custom resolvers applied once on behavior start"* · *"yes, switch to my model please. I need consistency."* ⭐⭐ **The model:** a BEHAVIOUR (root BTree/HSM, a hosted sub-behaviour, and — `O9` — a blueprint used as a behaviour) starts: clear → bake defaults → **stage 2 = the default plain copy of the SOURCE onto `In`, OR the resolver, which receives the SOURCE and writes whatever it chooses** (an empty body copies nothing). Source = the intent JSON (root) or the `ParamsVariable` host variable (sub-behaviour). An ACTION / CONDITION / HSM activity / HSM guard — C# or blueprint — reads its bound host variable in place every call; a host write mid-run is seen on the next call. A blueprint action keeps its own `WorkingState` (`Node` scope). ⛔ **Rules out:** a resolver on anything that is not a behaviour · a params copy for an action · a resolver run on top of an automatic copy. ⚠ **Partly built** — ✅ `CE-443` (resolver gets the source), ✅ `CE-445` (action-level resolvers + host accessor retired), ✅ `CE-444` (HSM activities/guards read live), all BUILT `2026-09-30`; `CE-444` (blueprint HSM activity/guard read live), `CE-445` (retire action-level resolvers), `CE-446` (blueprint as a behaviour). ✅ `IHostVariableAccess` retired too (user `2026-09-30`: *"Retire it."*, §P.6). ✅ A blueprint resolver's authored input = its declared **Parameters** (editor defaults), read with `GetAllParametersNode`; published as the behaviour's param schema; no nullable fields (user `2026-09-30`, §P.7). Amends `R-153`, `R-154`. 📄 [`DESIGN_Parameter_Model`](DESIGN_Parameter_Model.md) §P | `docs/blueprints/DESIGN_Parameter_Model.md` |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-156** | ✅✅ **THE BLUEPRINT WAY FIRST — STANDARD NODES AND BUILT-IN FUNCTIONS; A NON-GENERIC C# HELPER IS A LAST RESORT.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-30`, verbatim:** *"Non-generic c# helpers are a band aid and last resort, our desire is to represent those via standard blueprint nodes as much as possible."* · *"the arechitect rulings seems obsolete and needs revising."* ⭐ **Supersedes** `Q6-A` (demand-driven vocabulary) and the curated-helper leans of `Q6-B`/`Q6-C`; `Q6-D` (area query ≠ `EqsSensor`) is overtaken by the decision to **unify EQS into EQS 1.3**. ⭐ A GENERIC engine library surfaced in the palette (the `BlueprintMath` shape) counts as built-in; doctrine logic in C# does not. ⚠ `Q#5-C` (raw fixed buffers stay out of the graph, behind a curated accessor) is NOT revised. 📄 [`Q78`](Architect_Question_78_Hill_Attack_The_Blueprint_Node_Way.md) §6 — per-node costs and leans; [`HANDOFF_EQS_Unification`](batches/HANDOFF_EQS_Unification.md) |

| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-160** | ✅ **CREATOR OWNS ALL BUT OTHER ROLES' CLAIMS; A NON-CREATOR OWNS ONLY ITS ROLE'S CLAIM; ROLE CLAIMS NEVER OVERLAP.** 🔒 **User, `2026-10-01`:** *"if i am creator, i own all but the stuff other roles own. | ⭐⭐ **R-161** | ✅ **THE MAP2D ROLE CLAIMS NOTHING.** 🔒 **User, `2026-10-01`:** *"yes, map2d empty list"* — IG owns what it creates as creator, like any node. | [`Architect_Question_79`](Architect_Question_79_One_Ownership_Truth.md) §0.1 |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-162** | ✅ **SEVERAL NODES MAY HOLD THE SAME ROLE — "ONE NODE PER ROLE" IS WRONG; ⛔ SUPERSEDES R-157.** 🔒 **User, `2026-10-01`:** *"one node per role is wrong. Map2d is a role on multiple nodes already."* ⇒ ownership correctness needs, per entity, at most one node serving each role whose claim list is NON-EMPTY (the `ServesRole` shard contract, Role-Affinity §3.8); roles with empty lists (Map2D) are unconstrained. ⛔ No duplicate-role guard. | [`Architect_Question_79`](Architect_Question_79_One_Ownership_Truth.md) §0.1, §0.7 |

This grant concept must be ensured by any network imple,entation, makeing the ownership independet on the netowrk."* ⇒ ownership groups are defined once, network-agnostically; each network implementation maps its wire unit to them (BDC has no grant support today). | [`Architect_Question_79`](Architect_Question_79_One_Ownership_Truth.md) §0.7 G2/G6 |

Scenario needs editor picker fir terrain i guess"* ⇒ DotRecast in its own project; grids sized from the terrain bounds; SimHost infantry on the vehicle model for now; the stance runtime composed on the Muscle; terrain = a folder resolved by one `TerrainCatalog`, staged like the TKB, picked from the scenario menu, stamped into the header on save. ⚠ **As built (`2026-10-03`, ruling unchanged):** the grids are CHECKED not resized (`CE-3018`), the stance runtime moved to `CE-3010` — both recorded in the design's §7 rows. 📄 [`DESIGN_Terrain_World.md`](../DESIGN_Terrain_World.md) §7.2–§7.3 |

It could have special entity picker and automatic scenario load remapping"* → *"Approved, do all 3 steps."* ⇒ `Fdp.Toolkit.Replication.EntityRef` (8 bytes, the network id; JSON = the bare number); the TYPE drives the picker, the scenario-load remap (ONE type plan, `EntityRefRemap`), the resolve (`NetworkIdResolver`) and every palette; `[MapPickableEntity]` only narrows a pick. ⛔ `[RemapNetworkId]`, `BehaviorParamRemapperCompiler`, the Fdp.Presentation picker attribute and the extractor's hand-coded intent cases are deleted. ⭐ Authored data uses `EntityRef`; `Fdp.Core.Entity` stays for runtime state | [`DESIGN_Entity_Reference.md`](DESIGN_Entity_Reference.md) §3 |

| ⭐ **R-186** | ✅ **A BEHAVIOUR MAY CREATE A SENSOR OF ANY KIND (AQ82 M′).** 🔒 **User, `2026-10-04`:** *"Approved."* ⇒ its per-kind capability DTO (the TKB's own record) travels as JSON in the sensor's config sample, only on spawn/change; the same path overrides a TKB sensor; realism is a policy, not a hard refusal. | [`Architect_Question_82_One_Sensor_Form.md`](Architect_Question_82_One_Sensor_Form.md) §4 M |
