| ⭐ **R-216** | ✅ **HIT CHANCE — A DETERMINISTIC AIM DEFLECTION AT SPAWN (`AQ85`)**: A the bullet's direction is rotated by `θ = σ · d(k)` (σ = the shooter's dispersion, d(k) a fixed golden-ratio sequence over its shot count) and the EXISTING physics decides the hit — no dice, rails stay exact, range comes free; B on the Muscle in `FireProcessingSystem`; C σ = `WeaponMountDto.DispersionMils` (default 0 = exact aim, today) × 2 moving × 2 under fire (5 s) × stance (prone 0.5, crouched 0.75); D cover/target stance NOT in the first slice (3-D bullets vs walls are the buildings programme's fire half); E `UnderFire` stamped by the hit and near-miss producers; F the AI's weapon effectiveness × the SAME `HitChance`. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-07`:** *"AQ85 approved, go ahead"*. 📄 [`AQ85`](Architect_Question_85_Hit_Chance.md) §3 |
| ⭐ **R-217** | ✅ **PENETRATION ON THE LAUNCHER × AMMO PAIR, AND ONE RULE FOR ARMOUR AND WALLS (buildings §3d)**: P1 a round's penetration is looked up (loaded ammo, this weapon) → (ammo, generic `WeaponGuid 0`) → the mount's own `Penetration` (`CE-3071`) → 0 = unknown, on the existing `AmmoWeaponBallisticsDto` (+ `PenetrationMm`); P1b a mount names its loaded ammo (`WeaponMountDto.AmmoGuid`), switching later; P1c one constant per pair, a range table later; P2 bullets trace the terrain — a piece resists with material mm RHA/m × the path inside it, the round passes with `ArmorModel.PenetrationChance`, chances multiply into the damage (expected value, no dice), penetration is reduced by what was crossed, a crossing it cannot pass stops it; E1–E3 warheads/area effects are `CE-1032`, door Destroyed only. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-07`:** *"Penetration model approved"*. 📄 [`DESIGN_Building_Interiors.md`](../DESIGN_Building_Interiors.md) §3d, as built §3h |
| ⭐ **R-218** | ✅ **THE NAVMESH CHANGES AT RUNTIME — NEVER IN PLACE UNDER A QUERY.** STATE on fixed geometry (doors, smoke, costs) = the query filter reads live state; GEOMETRY (breach, crater, placed building) = copy-on-write: rebuild off-thread, swap ONE immutable snapshot. P1 the snapshot (built `2026-10-08`) · P2 tiled bake + tile rebuilder with `CE-1029` · P3 replan on a moved version with Building Interiors 5d. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-08`:** *"We will need to one day. How can we make it possible, prepare for that?"* → *"Approved"*. 📄 [`Navigation_Design_v2_0.md`](../designs/navig-2/Navigation_Design_v2_0.md) §14 |
| ⭐ **R-219** | ✅ **STATE THAT CHANGES AT RUNTIME IS READ FROM THE READER'S OWN VIEW — NEVER FROM A SHARED OBJECT.** A background module runs on a snapshot replica: per-entity components are COPIED into it, but a managed component without `SnapshotViaClone` and every SINGLETON are SHARED by reference (`SyncSingletonById`) ⇒ live state on such an object reaches every thread at once. Doors: `DoorState` entities are the only source; `TerrainWorld` holds none; every terrain/navmesh query takes `DoorStates.Of(view, terrain)` built from its caller's view. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-08`:** *"this is also sensitive topic to multi thread reading and writing. Is there an issue?"* → *"Approved, do the fix first"*. 📄 [`DESIGN_Building_Interiors.md`](../DESIGN_Building_Interiors.md) §3j 5b′ |
| ⭐ **R-220** | ✅ **A BACKGROUND BATCH ALLOCATES NOTHING PER QUERY (GC stutters).** A query that a background module or a per-tick system runs per candidate or per entity allocates 0 B per call once warm. Scratch is owned PER THREAD (two modules query one provider, `CE-2122`). A table handed to a caller is immutable. `DoorStates.Of` always RE-READS the door entities and reuses its previous table only when the bytes are equal: ⛔ never key it on a version, or a door written within the tick is served stale. DotRecast's A* node pool (one list per visited node) is the one allocation left; it is not ours, so the rail holds our queries to DotRecast's own cost for the same search (`CE-3108`). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-08`:** *"Are those background batches writeen in a garbage collection friendly way, to avoid gc stutters?"* → *"Approved, do it first"*. 📄 [`DESIGN_Terrain_World.md`](../DESIGN_Terrain_World.md) §6a |
| ⭐ **R-221** | ✅ **AN INTERACTION IS A TYPED FDP EVENT; IT CROSSES THE NETWORK ON ONE TOPIC WHOSE PAYLOAD IS A DDS UNION.** One event struct per interaction kind, its fields named for what they mean (readable in the bus inspectors, data breakpoints and the flight recorder); one `EntityInteractionRequest` topic with one union case per kind (a new kind extends the data model, never adds a topic or translator class); target and actor ride the header as network ids; the owner of what a kind changes applies it; the answer is the target's replicated state. ⛔ No binary-encoded parameters, on the bus or the wire. A cluster runs one build; an unknown kind is dropped. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-08`:** *"Wire compatibility matters. Dds fights this with data models."* · *"Fdp event with binary encoded data is opaque for anyone but the recipient."* 📄 [`DESIGN_Entity_Interactions.md`](../DESIGN_Entity_Interactions.md) |
| ⭐ **R-222** | ✅ **AN ENTITY'S NAVMESH LAYER IS MAPPED FROM ITS TKB LOCOMOTION CLASS ONTO THE FIXED BAKED LAYERS.** The bake makes a few layers, one per kind of mover (infantry, vehicle, …); a path request names one. `NavLayerSelection.For`: an explicit layer on the order wins; else `VehicleParams.Class` — `Pedestrian` ⇒ Infantry, any other ⇒ Vehicle. The class decides, not the size (a soldier opens doors and climbs stairs, a vehicle does neither). ⛔ `VehicleState` is not a vehicle marker (SimHost infantry carries it); ⛔ no per-entity runtime profile component for now. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-08`:** *"based on the true entity params (from TKB) to map to the exiting supported profile"* · *"Approved, go with class mapping"*. 📄 [`Navigation_Design_v2_0.md`](../designs/navig-2/Navigation_Design_v2_0.md) §8.3 · `CE-3112` |
| ⭐ **R-223** | ✅ **TEST BEHAVIOURS ARE C#; ONLY DEMOS FOR HUMANS ARE JSON ASSETS.** A node's logic is tested by calling its `[SharedAiAction]` method; an engine feature end to end uses a curated C# tree (`[BTreeDefinition(…, Curated = true)]` + `BTreeBuilder`, or `[HsmDefinition(…, Curated = true)]` + `HsmBuilder`, actions named with `nameof`) living in `Hrot.AI.Behaviors`; blueprint tests use the existing `BlueprintAssetBuilder`. JSON assets stay for demos a human watches or edits, and for tests of the asset pipeline itself. Production blueprints need no C# builder — C# is used directly. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-08`:** *"Only demos for human need to stay json assets. c# hardcoded behaviors of course need to live in Hrot.AI.Behaviors."* 📄 [`DESIGN_Behavior_Self_Registration.md`](DESIGN_Behavior_Self_Registration.md) §10b |
| ⭐ **R-224** | ✅ **A BOARDING IS ARBITRATED BY THE VEHICLE'S OWNER.** An embark/disembark/eject request travels as an interaction (R-221) to the vehicle's owner, which alone applies it to `PassengerBuffer`; that buffer replicates to every node, and each node DERIVES the passenger side (`IsEmbarkedTag`, the stripped capabilities) from it. ⛔ The actor's node never writes both entities. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-08`:** *"3110 lean approved"*. 📄 [`DESIGN_Entity_Interactions.md`](../DESIGN_Entity_Interactions.md) §7 Q-1 · `CE-3110` |
| ⭐ **R-225** | ✅ **A WARHEAD'S AREA EFFECT IS CALCULATED WHERE DIRECT-HIT DAMAGE IS, FROM THE AMMO TYPE, THROUGH THE TERRAIN MODEL.** The ammo type (on the shot, the detonation and the wire) resolves a `WarheadDto` (TKB → reference library → none); `AreaEffectSystem` beside `DamageCalculationSystem` takes the candidates in the radius, rates exposure per body point by terrain traces AND the shared 3-D collider test line of sight uses (fragments × `ArmorModel.PenetrationChance`, stopped by vehicles and bodies; blast in a diffraction SHADOW — not a block — behind an obstacle taller than the target's highest point; confinement/room-to-room v2 with Sound v2), and emits one `DamageAssessedEvent` per entity for the Health owner — no physics raycast, no new damage wire, no dice. Warhead rounds stopping on terrain detonate; indirect rounds fly an arc to a point. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-08`:** *"approved. How the explosion fragments hits will be detected"* · *"the physical based is the right way, as it is pretty normal to hide behind vehicles"* · *"approved, go ahead with stage 6"*. 📄 [`DESIGN_Building_Interiors.md`](../DESIGN_Building_Interiors.md) §3k |
| ⭐ **R-226** | ✅ **WHAT A DEBUG GIZMO DRAWS IS RECORDED ECS STATE — NEVER A SIDE OBJECT.** A debug layer reads a fixed-size component (`[DataPolicy(NoScenario)]`: recorded and snapshotted, not saved into scenarios, not replicated) that exists only where the module filling it lives, stamped with sim time; the gizmo fades by sim-time age. ⇒ a SEEK restores what the map shows (a detonation just before the target time is still in the ring), and the Replay Browser sees it. The text logs behind the routes stay; one mirror system per module copies them into the components. The Replay Browser loads the terrain by the NAME in the recording metadata (`CE-3118`); the exercise archive carries a COPY of the scenario, the terrain referenced by name only (`CE-3119`). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-08`:** *"Wouldnt it be simplier if we invented special ecs component carrying whatever state we need to visualize, that exist optionally only whwre thier filling modules live, and let them be saved as anything else?"* · *"Accepted."* · *"The replay browser must load the terrain in order to display it on the map if nothing else."* 📄 [`DESIGN_Terrain_Combat_Tuning.md`](../DESIGN_Terrain_Combat_Tuning.md) §5a |
| ⭐ **R-227** | ✅ **A MAP GIZMO'S SCOPE IS A RUNTIME SETTING PER FAMILY, AND A UNIT CAN BE PINNED.** The attribute declares the gizmo's family; `map.scope.<Family>` (All · SelectedOrPinned) is a runtime setting shown in the layer panel; a pin is a bit in the unit's `DebugState.Ai`, toggled from the map's entity context menu, applied by `DebugStatePatchSystem` on every host that pins (SimHost included); ONE policy decides (All, or selected, or pinned). The dormant AI overlay family is FOLDED into gizmos: its three duplicates and budget arbiter deleted, Utility and Squad rewritten as real gizmos. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-08`:** *"Sometimes i want the effect shown for selected entity only, sometimes i need to pin this gizmo 'enabled' to this entity because i need to see entity's gizmos temporarily even if entity not selected"* · *"Approved"* · *"① approved, fold them into gizmos"*. 📄 [`DESIGN_Terrain_Combat_Tuning.md`](../DESIGN_Terrain_Combat_Tuning.md) §5b |
| ⭐ **R-228** | ✅ **NO HOST-ONLY GIZMO WIRING — HAS DATA = CAN DRAW.** Beyond uniform gizmo membership, nothing gizmo-related is wired per host: debug toggles (pins, AI trace), their applying system and their component come from `MapInteractionPack` (the pack constructs, the host schedules `InteractionSystems`); a projector whose constructor needs a service gets it from `MapInteractionContext.Services` and is reported, never silently dropped, where the host lacks it; raw per-host map layers become gizmos; projectors live in shared assemblies; the completeness rail reads the deployed DLLs. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-08`:** *"Rhis should be unified and data driven (has data = can draw)"* · *"Approved, build all six"*. 📄 [`DESIGN_Uniform_Gizmo_Membership.md`](../DESIGN_Uniform_Gizmo_Membership.md) §10 |
| ⭐ **R-229** | ✅ **THE GEO ORIGIN COMES FROM THE TERRAIN, AND THE RECORDING KEEPS IT.** The terrain file specifies the origin and the geo-converter service takes it from there; a recording saves the origin in its metadata so a replay works even if the remembered terrain is gone. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-08`:** *"Geo origin should be specidied as part of terrain file and passed to geoconverter service from there. Origin can be saved in recording metadata in case terrain with remembered name no longer exists"*. 📄 [`DESIGN_Geo_Origin.md`](../DESIGN_Geo_Origin.md) (A, B revised by the user: no Berlin default, missing = 0,0,0; C–F approved) |
| ⭐ **R-230** | ✅ **THE NAVMESH AND THE ROAD NET ARE PARALLEL LAYERS; USING THE ROAD NET IS THE ACTOR'S PER-USE-CASE CHOICE.** The navmesh covers road areas too, independently of the road graph; the road graph is part of the terrain (its own file, `roadNetworks`); a road-net cost set per request decides whether an actor uses it (sneaking: no; transport over distance: yes, via navmesh → road → navmesh). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-08`:** *"Navmesh should be also where roadnet is, independently on it. Not all vehicles/people want to respect road net."* 📄 [`Navigation_Design_v2_0.md`](../designs/navig-2/Navigation_Design_v2_0.md) §5.2 |
| ⭐ **R-231** | ✅ **THE DANGER SENSOR READS THE ROAD GRAPH, AND THE ROAD POLYGONS RETIRE.** The danger-along-route classifier finds crossings and junctions on the road graph (segment bands, junction nodes), not on `surface: road` polygons; the world file's road polygons retire — the graph with its lane widths is the road. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-08`:** *"4 and 5 approved, write the CE-3128 design"* (CE-3128 ④ ⑤). 📄 [`Navigation_Design_v2_0.md`](../designs/navig-2/Navigation_Design_v2_0.md) §5.2a D7/D8 |
| ⭐ **R-232** | ✅ **STAGE 7a: BUILDING COVER COMES FROM EACH WALL'S FACES, PER STOREY; WINDOWS GIVE FIRING POSITIONS.** A building panel feeds the cover database by face at its storey's floor (kept only where that floor exists), doorways give nothing, a window's sill is a low wall, lintels never cover; window firing positions sit inside, facing out, at the lowest stance that clears the sill; solid prisms and free walls keep the old rule; computed at terrain load, never stored. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09`:** *"Look ok."* … *"Approved."* 📄 [`DESIGN_Building_Interiors.md`](../DESIGN_Building_Interiors.md) §3l C1–C9 |
| ⭐ **R-233** | ✅ **THE NAVMESH GETS A MAP LAYER, DRAWN WHERE IT EXISTS, ONE MESH PICKED BY A GIZMO SETTING.** Polygons of the host's own navmesh (SimHost, Editor), Infantry / Vehicle / all chosen in the gizmo's settings (default Infantry), door polygons by their door's state (⚠ *"by their flags"* in the proposal — doors are a Recast AREA and every polygon's flags are 1, so the state colours them; [`DESIGN_Terrain_Combat_Tuning.md`](../DESIGN_Terrain_Combat_Tuning.md) §5c N4); built after Stage 7a — ✅ BUILT `2026-10-09` (§5c). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09`:** *"Yes, navmesh layer after 7a as proposed"*. 📄 [`Blueprint_Issues_Tracker.md`](Blueprint_Issues_Tracker.md) CE-3133 |
| ⭐ **R-234** | ✅ **PEEK AND FIRE — THE WINDOW DUEL, D1–D13.** Aim at the highest SEEN body point (refines R-217); a person is hit only within the body band of his logical stance; an aimed shot needs the target seen now and an aim time (per weapon, fallback 0.8 s), blind fire has none; reload; the cover point's stance rides `EqsResult` (CE-3135); `ThreatExposureTest` revived; one `PeekAndFire` node (stance peek / step peek), position rotation, suppress-and-relocate, freshest evidence; street cover = static obstacle entities carrying a wall material from their TKB type. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09`:** *"Approved."* 📄 [`DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md`](../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md) §6 |
| ⭐ **R-235** | ✅ **UNIT-SCOPED SHARED BLACKBOARD STRUCTS ARE REQUIRED — re-implemented, not debated.** Per-unit memory that outlives a behaviour and is used only by behaviours is a DTO struct a designer declares in C#; it lives in the blackboard store, NOT as an ECS component per type (🔒 *"no automatic component allocations. Component id range is very limited. We can have hundresds of behaviors."*). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09`:** *"it is required to re-implement the unit-scoped shared blackboard structs"* — ⚠ the mechanism leans A–H are NOT ruled yet. 📄 [`Architect_Question_87_Unit_Memory.md`](Architect_Question_87_Unit_Memory.md) |
| ⭐ **R-236** | ✅ **THE OCCURRENCE STORE BECOMES MULTI-BLOCK AND NEVER MOVES A SLOT.** Growth appends the next existing tier as an extra block (no new component ids); allocated slots stay put, so an attach may grow the store mid-tick and a per-tick block view is safe; the copy-promotion retires. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09`:** *"new lean works"* (after *"the only thing needing to know where a data is is the one that looks up the byte pointer of the slot, no?"*). 📄 [`Architect_Question_87_Unit_Memory.md`](Architect_Question_87_Unit_Memory.md) §3 D″, §3a, §3b |
| ⭐ **R-237** | ✅ **UNIT MEMORY — Q87 LEANS A–G APPROVED.** A `[UnitMemory]` struct is one slot of kind `UnitMemory` in the unit's blackboard store, keyed by its TYPE (with a layout guard), created on first `Ref`/`Set` with `new T()` (a `Get` of an absent type creates nothing), living as long as the unit (never swept by a behaviour switch; recorded, not scenario-saved); access via two blueprint nodes `GetShared`/`SetShared` (wired fields only), a BTree/HSM `ref` param of that type, and `UnitMemory.Ref<T>`. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09`:** *"A-G approved, explain H"*. 📄 [`Architect_Question_87_Unit_Memory.md`](Architect_Question_87_Unit_Memory.md) §3 |
| ⭐ **R-238** | ✅ **PEEK AND FIRE BEHAVIOUR DETAIL B1–B8 APPROVED.** Used firing positions live in the unit memory `FiringPositionMemory` (8 slots); heat (+1.0 per exposure, +1.5 when shot at there, 45 s half-life), evaluated lazily from sim time; burned at 2.5, reusable below 1.0, penalised between; a candidate within 1 m is a slot, the coolest is replaced; pick from the cover sensor's 16 by score − heat penalty, non-burned, min relocate distance; aimed fire takes a round count; reloading = stay hidden. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09`:** *"approved"* (to B1–B8). 📄 [`../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md`](../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md) §8 |
| ⭐ **R-239** | ✅ **PEEK AND FIRE — THREE USER RULINGS `2026-10-09`.** ① **Aim point (D1 revised):** the middle when seen, or when a round goes through what hides it (≥ 50 % damage arrives); else the middle of the SEEN part of the body, between its lowest and highest visible heights — never the highest seen point. 🔒 *"aiming at highest point meant we wont hit, we need to aim in between the lowest seen body part and highest one"* · *"if the body is hidden behind a weak penetrable obstacle, we could aim to body center"*. ② **One solver per unit (P-4):** a new EQS solver pick prefers the node already solving a sibling sensor of the same unit (when it has the role) — 🔒 *"yes"*. ③ **Ammo meaning (D9):** `WeaponState.Ammo` stays the TOTAL carried; magazine rounds, magazine size and reload time are added beside it — 🔒 *"YES"*. 📄 [`../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md`](../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md) §6 D1, D9, §7 P-2, P-4 |
| ⭐ **R-240** | ✅ **"NO DEFAULT BERLIN" STILL APPLIES — SCENARIOS NAME A TERRAIN INSTEAD.** A node with no terrain keeps the geo origin at 0,0,0 (R-229 unchanged); a shipped scenario that named no terrain is CONVERTED to name the flat `berlin` terrain (the Berlin origin, no world). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09`:** *"'no default berlin' still applies - without terrain the geo reference zero; scenarios without terrain should be converted to scenarios with 'berlin' terrain."* 📄 [`../DESIGN_Geo_Origin.md`](../DESIGN_Geo_Origin.md) §4 ⑧ |
| ⭐ **R-241** | ✅ **AI ACTION STATUS ("THOUGHTS") APPROVED — T1–T4.** One recorded per-unit `ActionStatus` (a row per action channel: reason, progress, target), written by the EXECUTORS so every behaviour shows without edits; always present on brain units (not only while traced); drawn by the `Actions` gizmo family (selected or pinned by default, switchable, pinnable); behaviour-specific gizmos per behaviour (PeekAndFire with P-6). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09`:** *"AI's 'thoughts' approved."* 📄 [`../DESIGN_Ai_Action_Status_Gizmo.md`](../DESIGN_Ai_Action_Status_Gizmo.md) §2, §6 |
| ⭐ **R-242** | ✅ **P-7a STATIC OBSTACLES: A BOX FOOTPRINT AND FOUR TYPED STARTERS (O3, O4).** An obstacle's footprint is a box (length × width, the entity's yaw) × height from its TKB type, overridable per instance (`ObstacleShape`); four starter types — Car (`car-body`), Sandbag wall, Concrete block, Crate — and the Editor's zone obstacle becomes a Concrete block (fixes `CE-3141`). O1/O2/O5 (terrain rebuild, its timing, parked vehicles as live cover) still open. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09`:** *"O3, O4 accepted"*. 📄 [`../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md`](../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md) §9.2 |
| ⭐ **R-243** | ✅ **P-7a: STATIC OBSTACLES ARE TERRAIN GEOMETRY, GATED BY THE ENTITY LIFECYCLE; PARKED VEHICLES ARE LIVE COVER (O1, O5).** A static obstacle becomes a prism of its material in each node's terrain (bullets, sight, AI sight, cover, navmesh all read it). ⭐ It is not in the simulation until its bake has committed: the obstacle stays `Constructing` (lifecycle participant = the terrain module) and a scenario load is not finished while any entity is constructing ⇒ a scenario whose obstacles never change is deterministic whatever the machine's speed. A standing vehicle is cover read live (not baked). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09`:** *"scenario load should make sure the obstacles are baked (if they need baking) before scenario load is acknowledged finished. Entity lifecycle managemnt protocol is here exactly from this reason."* · *"O5 - OK"*. 📄 [`../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md`](../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md) §9.2 |
| ⭐ **R-244** | ✅ **R-143 EXEMPTION: A DEBOUNCE THAT ONLY SCHEDULES BACKGROUND WORK MAY USE THE WALL CLOCK.** The P-7a obstacle rebuild starts after a wall-clock quiet period (0.5 s) because it must also start while the cluster is paused or loading (sim time stands still); it decides nothing the simulation computes — WHEN an obstacle enters the simulation is decided by the entity lifecycle gate (R-243). ⛔ Not a licence for wall-clock simulation timing anywhere else. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09` (3rd round):** *"approved"* (to the proposal in [`../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md`](../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md) §9.2 O2) |
| ⭐ **R-245** | ✅ **P-7a O5: A VEHICLE IS "STANDING" (ITS SIDES ARE COVER POINTS) WHILE IT MOVES SLOWER THAN 0.3 m/s — NOW, NOT "STILL FOR 2 s".** No history is kept: the cover query reads the speed from its own view each time, so a car that drives off stops being cover on the next query. Supersedes O5 ②'s "has not moved for `ParkedSeconds` (2 s)". 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09`:** *"moving slower than 0.3 m/s is probably a better consition"*. 📄 [`../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md`](../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md) §9.7 |
| ⭐ **R-246** | ✅ **THE TOP BODY POINT IS THE EYE — "IF YOUR EYE SEES OUT, YOUR HEAD SHOWS".** A line of sight is one segment both ways, so a unit whose eye sees a target is seen by it. The person profile's top fraction is 1.0 of the stance's eye height (standing ≈ 1.7, crouched ≈ 1.1, prone ≈ 0.35 m), in the ONE profile — sight, the bullet body band (D2), fragments. Supersedes §3f's tops at 0.94 / 0.91 / 0.86 of the eye, which sat BELOW it: a man crouched at an upstairs window saw the street and was never seen from it. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09`:** *"if your eye sees out, your head shows" - yes sure like that, wasn't the body profile's top point (1.0 m) clearly wrong?* 📄 [`../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md`](../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md) D16 |
| ⭐ **R-247** | ✅ **A PERSON MOVES LIKE A PERSON, NOT A SMALL CAR — HE TURNS ON THE SPOT, THEN WALKS WHERE HE FACES.** Pedestrians are integrated by `HumanGait` (heading turns straight to the wanted direction, no turning circle; no walking while more than 30° off), every other class by the bicycle model. The motion model may be changed to suit human movement wherever it needs to. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-09`:** *"Feel free to modify the motion model to suite human movement. It has never been done only because not needed yet. Now it is."* 📄 [`../../FDP/Docs/projects/toolkits/FDP.Toolkit.CarKinem.md`](../../FDP/Docs/projects/toolkits/FDP.Toolkit.CarKinem.md) "Human gait" · [`../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md`](../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md) D17 |
| ⭐ **R-248** | ✅ **NEVER DESIGN FOR FLAT TERRAIN — THE FLAT GROUND IS A TEMPORARY SIMPLIFICATION.** `TerrainWorld`'s single `GroundZ` is to be replaced by real terrain height soon; every design (picking, draping, levels, spawn, sensors, navigation, rendering) must work over relief, and "level 0" means the ground surface at that point, never a constant height. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-10`:** *"Terrain needs height. Current flat terrain bed is unbearable. never count with terrain being flat, this is just current simplification that should be removed soon."* 📄 [`../DESIGN_Map_3D_Mode.md`](../DESIGN_Map_3D_Mode.md) §1 U23, §3.10 |
| ⭐ **R-249** | ✅ **GROUNDING IS THE ENTITY'S CLAMPING FLAG + ITS MOTION MODEL — NEVER A SPECIAL CASE FOR Z = 0.** A loaded entity keeps its saved Z; its motion model puts it on the surface as it moves, and its runtime ground-clamping flag (`GroundClampingConfig`, TKB default, DDS override) decides whether it is held to the surface at all. Terrain-height leans TH-A..TH-H approved. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-10`:** *"all approved. Whether entity should be ground clamped needs to be controlled by its 'ground clamping enabled' realtime flag and its motion model, not by adding exception for zero coordinate."* 📄 [`../DESIGN_Terrain_Height.md`](../DESIGN_Terrain_Height.md) §4a |
| ⭐⭐ **R-250** | ✅ **THE OWN TERRAIN, 3-D VIEW AND SIMHOST ARE A LIGHTWEIGHT DEVELOPMENT STAND-IN; PRODUCTION USES A MATURE 3-D ENGINE ⇒ EVERY ENGINE CAPABILITY THE EDITOR, MAP AND BRAIN RELY ON SITS BEHIND AN INTERFACE.** SimHost replaces the full engine (motion, perception, navigation…) with simpler alternatives; Stride was a step toward a full replacement, not the final engine. A concrete stand-in class used directly by a consumer is a seam gap. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-10`:** *"All the 3d engine stuff the editor and map and brain now relies on must be for sure abstracted behind interfaces to allow replacing later with another implementation using mature 3d engine."* 📄 [`../DESIGN_World_Query_Seam.md`](../DESIGN_World_Query_Seam.md) §1 |
| ⭐ **R-251** | ✅ **THE BEPU LIBRARY IS A VALID CONTENDER — THE EARLIER "TOO YOUNG" WAS ABOUT STRIDE'S BEPU INTEGRATION.** The `2026-08-22` ruling (Stride stays on Bullet) is about Stride only. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-10`:** *"My bepu statement was about bepu support in stride, not the bepu library itself. it is definitely a valid contender here."* 📄 [`../DESIGN_World_Query_Seam.md`](../DESIGN_World_Query_Seam.md) WQ-D |
| ⭐⭐ **R-252** | ✅ **THE STAND-IN TERRAIN IS AN APPROXIMATION OF THE FINAL ENGINE'S — THE BRAIN AND THE ENGINE INTERFACES MAY NOT ASSUME ITS SIMPLIFICATIONS.** No 2.5-D extrusions, no single ground, no fixed material list, no `Z = 0` meaning "on the ground", no caller-supplied door states; the interface speaks heights, surfaces and per-purpose traces only. Extends `R-248`. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-10`:** *"brain should not assume some simplification resulting from the fact the current terrain is simple; it will not be later."* 📄 [`../DESIGN_World_Query_Seam.md`](../DESIGN_World_Query_Seam.md) §2a, §3.3 |
| ⭐ **R-253** | ✅ **WORLD-QUERY SEAM WQ-A..WQ-H APPROVED; THE LIBRARY IS CHOSEN ON SIMPLICITY OF USE AND PURE C#, NOT ON THE .NET VERSION.** HROT may move to a newer .NET when needed, so a binding's target framework is no argument; interop cost and native packaging are. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-10`:** *"approved. Regarding physics library choice, the simplicity of use is also a criteria. We could move HROT to c# 10 if we want to … pure c# is a big advantage"*. 📄 [`../DESIGN_World_Query_Seam.md`](../DESIGN_World_Query_Seam.md) §4a |
| ⭐ **R-254** | ✅ **SHARED FROM DAY ZERO — A CAPABILITY EVERY HOST SHOULD HAVE IS BUILT IN THE ONE SHARED CONSTRUCTION PATH, SO "NOT ON ALL HOSTS" CANNOT HAPPEN BY CONSTRUCTION.** A per-host rollout slice is a smell: put it in the shared pack, and rail that every host goes through it. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-10`:** *"we should be unifying and sharing from the day zero so something like 'not on all host' can not happen by construction"* — applied to the 3-D map (`MapInteractionPack.AttachMapLayers`, S6 withdrawn). | [`DESIGN_Map_3D_Mode.md`](../DESIGN_Map_3D_Mode.md) §6b |
| ⭐ **R-255** | ✅ **THE TURRET IS A PART; THE WEAPON LOGIC AIMS IT.** A turret is a part child (`PartMetadata`) carrying its pose (azimuth relative to the hull, gun elevation relative to the turret); the WEAPON side turns it — it knows how to aim, within TKB limits and rates; the brain only names the target; it replicates through the NED/BDC multi-instance descriptor pattern the sensor sub-entities already use. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-10`:** *"M24 - yes the weapon side (the weapon logic) moves the turret - knows how to aim, brain just specifies the target"* · *"M26 - solved already by multi-instance descriptors in NED/BDC (similar stuff as already used for sensor sub-entities)"* | [`DESIGN_Map_3D_Mode.md`](../DESIGN_Map_3D_Mode.md) §3.11 |
| ⭐ **R-260** | ✅ **THE MAP CAMERA IS ONE SCENARIO ENTITY — "the view the scenario opens in".** One per scenario (not per host), owned by the node that SAVES the scenario (never an IG, R-140); map hosts adopt its pose and 2-D/3-D mode when the scenario opens and only REQUEST moves. Its pose is the ordinary `SimTransform` (the eye + yaw/pitch, U7), the mode is derived — straight down = 2-D. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-11`:** *"S5 approved."* (on the three leans of §3.12) | [`DESIGN_Map_3D_Mode.md`](../DESIGN_Map_3D_Mode.md) §3.12 |
| ⭐ **R-261** | ✅ **THREE WEAPON LAYERS: the behaviour brain says WHAT to attack, the WEAPON BRAIN (Brain node, a hardcoded system per weapon category) picks weapon and ammo and keeps ROE / seen-now / magazine, the WEAPON MUSCLE (Muscle node) keeps the gun aimed — ballistics sets the elevation — and fires the rounds it was granted.** Brain → Muscle is a standing per-mount `WeaponTask`, Muscle → Brain a `WeaponTaskStatus`; turrets travel as a multi-instance descriptor keyed by turret index, the pose Muscle-owned; the simplest profile keeps the per-shot path with `AimSeconds`. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-11`:** *"Weapon brain should be something out of usual higher level behavior, doing low level weapon logic based on weapon channel requests."* + W-1..W-9 *"approved."* | [`DESIGN_Weapon_Layers.md`](../DESIGN_Weapon_Layers.md) §4 |
| ⭐ **R-262** | ✅ **BRIDGES OVER A HEIGHT GRID ARE A DECK SLAB AND TWO RAMPS — no imported meshes.** Walkables take an optional ground-relative height (`aboveGround`); a ramp's foot is BURIED (extended past the bank, sunk below the ground) so the navmesh and the movement model join it to the ground by construction; movers drive by `SurfaceZ`, never the navmesh, and a road leg's reported Z follows the same rule. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-11`:** *"The requirement is for bridges mainly (could be a simple rectangular ramp) so the cars/people are not driving on height grid only. No import of complex 3d meshes."* + TH-J / TH-K *"Approved."* | [`DESIGN_Terrain_Height.md`](../DESIGN_Terrain_Height.md) §3.4 |
| ⭐ **R-256** | ✅ **THE BEHAVIOUR LIBRARY GETS A WRITTEN LINE, AND PEEK-AND-FIRE IS GENERALISED BEFORE IT JOINS (review L1–L4).** L1 a Library section + a promotion rule (no scenario constant, stated unit scope, a rail outside its demo, one implementation per concept) · L2 PeekAndFire is generalised (exposure model, failure paths, ROE/friendly-line/mount on bursts, one-threat coherence, squad de-confliction + seed; scope infantry), THEN bound to CombatPosture's TakeCover (CE-3157) · L3 legacy cover nodes stay out of the library, only the dead are deleted · L4 the behaviour registry is fixed (tree-less names, missing library names) · ✅ L5 (approved later the same day): `FireAtTarget` routes onto the one aimed fire step `PostureNodes.Fire`. 📄 [`REVIEW_Behaviour_Library_Genericity.md`](../REVIEW_Behaviour_Library_Genericity.md) §4/§5 |
| ⭐ **R-257** | ✅ **PEEK-AND-FIRE GENERALISED, SQUAD-AWARE (G1–G8, `DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md` §10).** G1 peek from the cover POINT (`EqsResult.Kind`; hide at its stance, peek one taller; else step) · G2 honest `Failure` after `NoCoverSeconds`, unreachable ⇒ burned, all burned ⇒ aimed fire from here · G3 friendly line on direct bursts (indirect exempt), HoldFire never exposes, ReturnFire only in the window · G4 one target locked per exposure (squad assignment, else TopThreat) through `PostureNodes.Fire(target)` · G5 per-unit `CoverClaim` ECS component, first claim wins, same force · G6 `MayExpose` seam for the squad sequencer (CE-507) · G7 `SimRng` mixes, not XOR · G8 infantry scope, generic defaults, `Disable` flags. 🔒 User: *"approved"*. 📄 [`DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md`](../DESIGN_Peek_And_Fire.md) §10 |

Supersedes, for the library, `DESIGN_Decision_Layer.md` §3.3c/§3.3d parity. 📄 [`REVIEW_Behaviour_Library_Genericity.md`](../REVIEW_Behaviour_Library_Genericity.md) §5 |
| ⭐ **R-259** | ✅ **ONE RETREAT IN THE LIBRARY: `FallBack`; `FleeExecutor` IS LEGACY (review L7).** `FallBack` (EQS safe-retreat point, one pathed move) is the library retreat the posture's Flee option runs; `FleeExecutor` (flee vector, no cover, registered on no CGF) stays in code, marked legacy, out of the library (L3 rule). ⚠ Revisit only if `FallBack` needs a no-cover escape. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-10`:** *"the still open one approved, go"*. | [`REVIEW_Behaviour_Library_Genericity.md`](../REVIEW_Behaviour_Library_Genericity.md) §5/§6 |
| ⭐ **R-263** | ✅ **OPERATIONS LIBRARY — D1–D5, D7 DECIDED (catalogue §10.1).** Humans: injury states ok / wounded / incapacitated / dead + bleed-out. Vehicles: **mobility kill · firepower kill · destroyed**, preferably DERIVED from a per-compartment damage model. Drones: point-mass flight + acoustic signature + battery, **following a planned 3-D path and attacking autonomously** when a suitable target is spotted. Civilians: crowd agents + a per-person **HSM** (*"our HSMs are optimized for large crowds"*) + a crowd-level system. Suspicion = hidden truth + observable cues. Melee = simple contact + `ThrowAt`. Trenches = authored polylines carved into height grid + navmesh. ⏳ D6 (= `CE-507` D2/D3) and D8 open. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-11`.** | [`DESIGN_Operations_Behaviour_Catalogue.md`](../DESIGN_Operations_Behaviour_Catalogue.md) §10.1 |

Library HSMs may be fluent-built in C# in `Hrot.AI.Behaviors` — no JSON asset. D3: autonomous `ManeuverSelect` only after a real danger-area provider. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-11`.** | [`DESIGN_Squad_Wiring.md`](../designs/group-maneuvers/DESIGN_Squad_Wiring.md) §5.1 |

Timed entities carry `Lifespan { BornAtSimSeconds, Seconds }` and ONE system removes them (`EffectLifetime` retires). Effect entities are RECORDED (never saved to a scenario) and the spawners are off in replay — a replay jump shows the right effects (supersedes VE-B "not recorded"). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-11`:** *"Timed effect could have component stating its lifespan and time of creation (simtime)"* · *"If recorded, replay will show effects properly after jump in time."* | [`DESIGN_Visual_Effects.md`](../DESIGN_Visual_Effects.md) §7 |

Trait component needs some defaults to never come up unfilled/zeroed as that would ruin the behavior."* | [`Architect_Question_89_Suppress_And_Close.md`](Architect_Question_89_Suppress_And_Close.md) |
| ⭐ **R-270** | ✅ **ENTITY CONTROL IS AN ORDER OF HIGH ENOUGH ORIGIN, NEVER AN AI-OFF SWITCH; HTTP, MENU AND RTS ORDERS GO THROUGH ONE SERVICE; FPS IS POSSESSION BY ONE STATION.** HTTP orders (origin Operator) come first, then the menu (Move / Engage with a follow-up point / entity pick) and an RTS tool, then FPS. FPS runs in our 3-D map view; the operator overrides the ROE; one station per unit, FPS only (a second station takes over); release resumes the earlier task, an order replaces it; "attack" closes in and fights (CombatPosture with a designated target); ammo crosses the network for the HUD; one operator per node; the AI player sees only what its side knows and may be locked to one side. FPS motion is a DIRECT manual-drive command (people strafe and turn on the spot, vehicles steer, free look with an optional hull-locked view), not direct-to-point; the generic `/entities/command` route is fixed. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-11`:** *"Your leans accepted."* — the motion M-1..M-10 leans were approved later the same day (R-271 (backend)). | [`DESIGN_Entity_Control.md`](../DESIGN_Entity_Control.md) §2 |
| ⭐ **R-271 (backend)** | ✅ **ENTITY CONTROL M-1..M-10 APPROVED, AND ALL ITS MESSAGING IS FDP BUS EVENTS — THE NETWORK IS ONLY A TRANSLATOR, SO EVERYTHING WORKS IN THE NETWORKLESS EDITOR.** Manual drive is its own Brain-group `ManualDrive` + `KinematicsMode.Manual` (people strafe and turn on the spot with body yaw = camera yaw; vehicles throttle / brake / reverse / steer, free look with `V` hull-lock; aircraft deferred); a person's step is refused on unwalkable navmesh; possess / release / control frames are FDP events carried between nodes as interaction cases; `BehaviorOrigin.Possession` pauses and resumes the task; operator fire reuses `AimAndFireExecutor`; the FPS camera is a driver on `MapCamera3D`; the menu orders are registered once in `MapInteractionPack`. A sender publishes on its own bus, the owner's handler reads its own bus, egress translators forward only what this node does not own; each slice is railed FIRST in the editor world. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-11`:** *"Approved. All the messaging needs to work in the networkless editor so ig needs using fdp bus events translated to network and back, accepting the latency."* | [`DESIGN_Entity_Control.md`](../DESIGN_Entity_Control.md) §2.2, §2.3 |

| ⭐ **R-272 (backend)** | ✅ **A UNIT'S WEAPON POSTURE — AT EASE · READY · AIMED — IS WEAPON STATE, CONTROLLED BY THE WEAPON BRAIN.** The weapon brain's executors write `WeaponPosture` where they decide aiming / firing / holding, its dispatcher lowers it when idle (Ready at once, AtEase after 5 s); it is Brain-owned and replicated (`dtWeaponPosture`) so every host draws the same; no viewer derives it from fire events and nothing reads the diagnostic `ActionStatus` for it. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-11`:** *"the aiming/at ease needs to be weapon state controlled from the weapon brain. if not there we need to define and add"* | [`DESIGN_Weapon_Layers.md`](../DESIGN_Weapon_Layers.md) W-13 |

The carry mode lives in the passenger list and on the wire like the seat; the carrier starts the act; the carrier cannot fire while carrying; a downed carrier drops the casualty alive. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-13`: "I1-I6 approved, C5-C10 approved, go ahead with CE-2155".** | [`../DESIGN_Injury_States.md`](../DESIGN_Injury_States.md) §6 · [`../DESIGN_Entity_Interactions.md`](../DESIGN_Entity_Interactions.md) §7.4.8 |

Rescue is a 4th option of the squad contact decision, a weighted product of urgency (bleed-out), exposure (pooled contacts that see the casualty and the cover point), quiet (since last under fire) and fire superiority, re-decided every 5 s while a man is down; a medic still walking returns to cover when fire picks up, a carrying one keeps going. Fire and movement: halves first, pairs later. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-13`: "Leans approved." · "Approved, write it into the design, do not start imolementing".** | [`../designs/group-maneuvers/DESIGN_Squad_Tactics.md`](../designs/group-maneuvers/DESIGN_Squad_Tactics.md) §7.5, §7.6 |

The technicals 104 / 105 and the soldiers 2006 / 2007 become presets. ★ **W-I:** a preset seats its CREW too (`StartWith` on any seat) — 104 / 105 arrive with a real gunner; orders go to the host; killing the gunner silences the gun. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-14`:** *"Automatic turret is required. | ⭐ **R-2037** | ✅ **THE BRAIN NEVER READS WORLD GEOMETRY.** Line of sight, building geometry and anything the world can change (smoke, destruction, doors, surfaces) is ASKED of the muscle — a sensor or a raycast / area query that goes to the muscle and comes back as a result; never computed on the brain from its own copy of the terrain. The DECISIONS over the answer stay on the brain. ⇒ planning that needs the world is a round trip with a wait (ask → wait → decide), not a single call. ⭐ The terrain loaded on the brain is for DRAWING THE MAP, never for a decision; every existing brain-side query is a TRACKED defect (`CE-2286` behaviors, `CE-2287` backend's sites). 🔒 *"The terrain on the brain exists mainly just for rendering it on the map."* 🔒 **User, `2026-10-18`:** *"The line of sight must always be a sensor or raycast query which goes to the muscle and returns back as a result. It is nothing that is calculatable immediately on the brain It always needs to go to muscle and back."* | [`DESIGN_Defend_And_Attack_Position.md`](../designs/group-maneuvers/DESIGN_Defend_And_Attack_Position.md) §13b |
| ⭐ **R-2038** | ✅ **SECTORS AT TWO LEVELS (DA-24) · THE SURVEY IS A STANDING SENSOR · ROOMS ARE DERIVED BY THE SENSOR.** A squad's sector is an AREA ENTITY polygon (R-2003) made by the platoon or drawn by the operator; a man's sector is an ARC in his order made by the squad; NoFire areas are looked up through the chain of command. The muscle's survey (DA-25) is a STANDING sensor re-solved in the background — the brain reads the latest answer without re-asking or waiting; re-asking is for NEW INPUTS. The room graph is derived by the sensor itself from the building's current geometry — never provided by the scenario or the terrain — so any building works without prior knowledge. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-18`:** *"Da 24 approved."* · *"It shouldn't be something the scenario or terrain needs to provide. That way we could apply the behavior to any building without prior knowledge."* | [`DESIGN_Defend_And_Attack_Position.md`](../designs/group-maneuvers/DESIGN_Defend_And_Attack_Position.md) §13a, §13b |

A person is aimed at his CHEST (his pose's torso, part way to its head — the boxes his hits are taken on); a chest seen just over a cover that stops a round ⇒ the middle of what shows, never lower; the muscle judges the masked muzzle on the line the round flies. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-20`:** *"if attacking is desired, then the spot must let him shoot. | ⭐⭐ **R-2069** | ✅ **THE PLATOON RIDES A CONVOY ROUTE AS A TASK (`PlatoonConvoy`, `CE-2347`, DA-99…DA-103).** Move: the squads ride the vehicles they sit in, the vehicles follow the route; Contact: the squads' own SOP fights, the platoon orders no squad and the vehicles drive on as they do (a carrier pushes through itself, CE-2307) — only a vehicle whose squad got out waits for it; the platoon's hold still pauses the task and leaves a fight alone; Clear: ~30 s with no live enemy seen and nobody fighting ⇒ the dismounted squads secure at their vehicles; Remount: each brings its wounded in and boards its own vehicle — one that cannot move is left, its squad takes another with room, else follows on foot — and the convoy carries on from where it stands. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-21`:** *"yes"* — to *"the vehicles keep following the convoy route as they do now … the squads secure the ambush site around their vehicles, report Set (in position), and carry their wounded … its squad boards the other vehicle if there is room or follows on foot."* | [Defend and Attack §11a](../designs/group-maneuvers/DESIGN_Defend_And_Attack_Position.md) (`CE-2347`) |
| ⭐⭐ **R-2070** | ✅ **THE SOLDIER'S COVER REACTION FIGHTS FROM COVER AND ENDS AFTER A QUIET LAPSE (AQ98 A–D).** A: `BasicInfantrySop`'s hit / near-miss / first-threat rows react with `FightFromCover` (= `PeekAndFire`, ROE-gated), not the hide-only `TakeCover`; B: the reaction ends once he is in cover with no hit / near miss on him for 8 s — the task resumes (amends Decision Layer §4.7's "while a threat is remembered"); C: a man with no task who sees an enemy reacts the same way; D: the weapon status row clears when no weapon action runs. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-21`:** *"Aq 98 approved."* | [AQ98 §3](Architect_Question_98_Cover_Reaction_Fights_Back.md) (`CE-2331`, `CE-2332`, `CE-2333`) |

| ⭐ **R-3001** | ✅ **AN ASSET PICKS ITS PARAMS DTO (C# OR `.params.json`) AND OVERRIDES ITS DEFAULTS PER FIELD** — the editor offers every registered params DTO, hand-written or generated; the asset's `ParamsDefaultJson` holds only the fields it overrides (edited with StructEdit), baked over the DTO's own defaults; blueprints get the same field. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-13`: "the assent must be able to override them (shared DTO for multiple assets might need different defaults per asset)".** Tracker `CE-3194` (backend half) + `HANDOFF_Params_Panels_Ui` U5 (editor half). | [`../DESIGN_Orders_Params_Entity_Classes.md`](../DESIGN_Orders_Params_Entity_Classes.md) §13.10a |
| ⭐ **R-3002** | ✅ **A PARAMS DTO'S OWN DEFAULTS ARE ITS FIELD INITIALISERS — NEVER AN ATTRIBUTE.** A C# struct DTO declares initialisers + a parameterless constructor; the `.params.json` generator emits the same; `[ParamsDto(DefaultJson)]` retires; every reader takes `new T()` (the bake at run time, the editor by `Activator.CreateInstance`). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-13`: "Param Dto defaults od csharpstruct should be written as initializers, not as attributes."** Refines R-3001; tracker `CE-3194`. | [`../DESIGN_Orders_Params_Entity_Classes.md`](../DESIGN_Orders_Params_Entity_Classes.md) §13.10a |
| ⭐ **R-3003** | ✅ **A SOLDIER'S SHOT WAITS FOR HIS BODY, AND HIS BODY FOLLOWS A LASTING AIM.** A person fires an aimed round only within `HumanGait.MaxAimTwist` (75°, the drawn twist) of his heading — a snap shot; beyond it the round is held while the Muscle's gait turns him at full rate, and inside it the gait aligns the body smoothly (a first-order lag) while the aim lasts; moving while aimed he keeps his path and back-pedals / sidesteps. The weapon brain never writes the heading (R-247); the gait reads the replicated `WeaponPosture`. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-14`: "Firing with high angle difference should be possible (like quick fire on suddenly appearing target) but i think The motion should try to align the body smoothly to the firing direction if that situation (target behind) lasts longer"**. Tracker `CE-3195`. | [`../DESIGN_Weapon_Layers.md`](../DESIGN_Weapon_Layers.md) W-14
| ⭐ **R-3004** | ✅ **A RETREAT ALWAYS ANSWERS.** `FindSafeRetreatPoint` keeps its hard *hidden from the top threat* filter, but when it rejects EVERY point (open ground) the whole set is ranked instead — least exposed, farthest from the threat, reachable — so a squad breaking contact and a soldier falling back never freeze in the open. The template's grid widens its spacing to cover the radius it is asked for, and "farther from the threat" is scaled over the distances the candidates can actually have (it saturated when the threat stood beyond the radius). Supersedes Squad Tactics S2-F's *"no answer ⇒ hold"* for the open-ground case. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-14`: "Approved"** (CE-2164). | [`EQS_Design_v1.3_final.md`](../designs/eqs-2/EQS_Design_v1.3_final.md) §19.6a |
| ⭐ **R-3005** | ✅ **ONE MUSCLE ROLE MOVES EVERY BODY — GROUND, AIR, LATER WATER.** `MuscleGround` keeps its name and owns every mover's position (`dtWorldPos`, R-170); its group applies to a template with car kinematics OR flight parameters, and a flyer's flight state sits in it. There is no `MuscleAir` / `MuscleNaval` role: the motion domains are modules (Motion Domains MD-B's first half stands) that all run on the node holding `MuscleGround`. Amends MD-B; closes `CE-3199`. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-14`: "lets drop the whole MuscleXXX (ground, air...) and use one single Muscle role, so all positions are owned by it, as now" · "keep MuscleGround so we do not need to rename".** | [`../designs/motion-models/DESIGN_Motion_Domains.md`](../designs/motion-models/DESIGN_Motion_Domains.md) §5a |
| ⭐ **R-3006** | ✅ **A VEHICLE'S PLANNED CORNERS ARE ROUNDED FOR ITS TURN RADIUS — AND IT FOLLOWS THEM.** The solver replaces each corner of a vehicle's path by an arc of radius WheelBase / tan(MaxSteer) through the planned vertex (shifted out where the navmesh has room); the follower's lookahead shrinks across a curve so the arc is driven, not cut. Not a wider bake, not a follower-only fix. Tracker `CE-3029`. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-14`: "round each corner in the planned path to fit the vehicle's turning radius."** | [`../designs/navig-2/Navigation_Design_v2_0.md`](../designs/navig-2/Navigation_Design_v2_0.md) §5.2b |
| ⭐ **R-3007** | ✅ **AN AIRCRAFT CRASH IS NOT A WARHEAD; EACH FLYER TYPE ACCELERATES AT ITS OWN RATE; A PLANE'S THROTTLE IS HELD; THE MK-82 IS A LINE IN `ammo.json`.** Only a flyer that IS a munition (`Gen.Flight DetonatesOnImpact`, the FPV drone) bursts its warhead on impact; any other aircraft is destroyed and comes to rest. `Gen.Flight AccelMps2` per type (FPV 15, UH-60 3; 0 ⇒ the model's own) on routes, point moves and manual flight — keys released ⇒ it brakes to a hover and holds. W/S change the plane's speed and release keeps it (any speed in [stall, top]). The reference library stays embedded JSON (the 2026-10-07 data-not-code choice). Tracker `CE-3201`, `CE-3202`. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-14`: "keep json, accel yes, throttle yes"** · *"Accepting your lean for the aircraft crash. Accepting adding the MK82 bomb."* | [`../designs/air-attack/DESIGN_Air_Operations.md`](../designs/air-attack/DESIGN_Air_Operations.md) §6b |
| ⭐ **R-3008** | ✅ **THE ONE FRAGMENT LAW'S NUMBERS STAND (CE-3203 C1–C5).** Body armour stops fragments over 35 % of a man (the plates, not the head and limbs); each weapon type's area / casing / health are the starter values in Weapon Systems §5b (guided launchers and the sentry the fragile ones); a shaped charge fragments 2 % of its body (the PG-7V at its public 2 / 5 m radii); a weapon carried by a soldier is struck with him (his carry station is open); the 25 / 30 mm HE numbers stand, unrailed until a published radius exists. Tracker `CE-3203`. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "approved C1-C5"** | [`../DESIGN_Building_Interiors.md`](../DESIGN_Building_Interiors.md) §3k.1 · [`../designs/weapons/DESIGN_Weapon_Systems.md`](../designs/weapons/DESIGN_Weapon_Systems.md) §5b |
| ⭐ **R-3009** | ✅ **BLUEPRINT STATE IS ALWAYS INSPECTABLE BY NAME.** The replay browser's merged view carries every blueprint blackboard component each entity has (an entity holds a LIST of them — growth appends a larger block, R-236), taken from the node that owns it, so the blueprint panels decode it; a JSON dump (inspector, debug API, replay diff/export) shows blueprint state DECOMPILED — variables by name — ⛔ never raw bytes. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`:** *"shouldnt we simply replaying all blueprint blackboard components that are currently present?"* · *"json dumping raw byte make absolutely no sense so "decompiled" version is desired"* | [`Blueprint_Issues_Tracker.md`](Blueprint_Issues_Tracker.md) `CE-3204` |
| ⭐ **R-3010** | ✅ **HIT LOCATION: ONE PART LIST FOR DRAWING, COLLIDING AND DAMAGE.** The kit's geometry is shared by the renderer and the hit test; a round is tested grid → bounding sphere → parts; every part has a fixed id per body plan; zones are sets of parts bound to damage specs; one `PartDamage` (Brain) serves wounds and vehicle compartments; `HitMarks` (a fixed ring, Muscle, part-local) carries where rounds struck; people are slice 1; zones are defined once per DIS class (a body profile) and overridden sparsely per type (HL-12). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`:** *"Especially people will benefit from more precise collisions, i need it in slice 1"* · *"Zone = set of bones"* · *"approved, write the design."* | [`DESIGN_Body_Parts_And_Hit_Location.md`](../designs/hit-location/DESIGN_Body_Parts_And_Hit_Location.md) `CE-3209`–`CE-3212` |
| ⭐ **R-3011** | ✅ **EVERY TKB MUNITION CARRIES ITS FULL SISO DIS MUNITION TYPE — AS IDENTITY, NOT AS THE PHYSICS KEY.** The DIS / HLA gateway is planned, so the round fired must be nameable in a Fire / Detonation PDU: each built-in munition gets its SISO-REF-010 code from a cited enumeration (an unknown field 0, never invented). Ammo physics defaults stay matched by name and the generator's driving parameters (calibre, warhead, mass). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`:** *"Dis/hla gateway is planned, so dis type for ammo in tkb is desired."* | [`DESIGN_Terrain_Combat_Tuning.md`](../DESIGN_Terrain_Combat_Tuning.md) §2b · `CE-3213` |
| ⭐ **R-3012** | ✅ **WEATHER IS REGIONAL DATA ON SCENARIO ENTITIES; TIME OF DAY IS THE SIM CLOCK PLUS AN OFFSET.** One positionless World Environment entity (no `SimTransform`) carries the `ScenarioClock` (UTC = ticks at sim zero + sim time; no day rate) and the GLOBAL weather; Weather Region area entities carry the same weather component and blend in by the centroid-to-edge distance along the polygon (1 at the centroid, 0 at the edge), overlapping regions adding, the global filling the rest; wind blends as a vector. The clock's value IS the scenario start time — authored in the scenario's Details view, saved as authored (never from the wall clock, never rewritten at save), and a runtime change just sets a new start time; humidity and precipitation are data. Every derived quantity is a pure function on every node (`WeatherField`). | `docs/designs/weather/DESIGN_Weather_And_Time.md` |
| ⭐ **R-4000** | ✅ **ONE CODE PATH ON EVERY NODE — A BEHAVIOUR THAT MUST DIFFER IS DECIDED BY THE NODE'S ROLE, NEVER BY A MISSING HANDLER.** Every node registers the same cluster handlers (recording, replay, save, load …); a host bootstrap never omits one to switch a behaviour off. When a node must act differently, its role decides — the role declares the WHAT, the host supplies the HOW (the §3.2 load-phase shape) — and the role → behaviour map is application code, never FDP (`NodeRole.cs`, 2026-09-12). Prefer a more robust shared path to a per-role exception. Precedent: IG registers the same replay/live handlers and differs only in the controller it hands them (`ListenerRecordReplayController`). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "So if we want to disable it, we need to do it via roles so the code stays unified."** · *"if we make stuff more robust, we could stay largely unified"*. Related: `CE-279` (unified handler registration; A2 deferred). | [`../DESIGN_Node_Roles_And_Policies.md`](../DESIGN_Node_Roles_And_Policies.md) §3.3 |
| ⭐ **R-4001** | ✅ **THE HEARTBEAT IS THE ONLY TIME LIMIT IN THE CLUSTER PROTOCOL, AND IT MUST KEEP BEATING THROUGH ANY VALID LONG OPERATION.** A node leaves a round only when its heartbeat stops (then it is removed from every in-flight round); no per-round or per-ACK timer may fail a valid long load or transfer. Long work never blocks the frame thread: the frame thread COORDINATES (polls status and progress each frame), and only work that touches no shared data — building a new private object — runs on a worker; every write into shared state happens in a short frame-thread Commit (user, same day: "safer would be to still coordinate from main thread, make the loading operations non-blocking"). A stuck-but-alive operation is reported (progress) and cancelled by an operator, never timed out. Also approves Q91 (the Player node = an IG node; authority derived from simulation roles; late join by replaying existing ops). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "accepted." · "time limits should be based on heartbeat and the heartbeat must keep working during long loading operations so timeouts can never break a valid long load or other operation."** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-B, §4-B2 |
| ⭐ **R-4002** | ✅ **FILE TRANSFER TO A NODE GOES THROUGH ONE PROVIDER INTERFACE — non-blocking, reports completion, progress and a status code + message — WITH THE TRANSPORT BEHIND IT.** Two transports: a share the node advertises in its greeting, and a DDS transfer (offer → "already cached?" reply → chunks → done) for files that are not GBs. The cache check reuses the existing (length, mtime) diff. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "in any way i would like to hide the transfer type behind a provider interface that support non-blocking operation and reports when finished and support progress reporting and error code/message status."** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-E2 |
| ⭐ **R-4004** | ✅ **AN OPERATOR CANCEL RETURNS THE CLUSTER TO `Idle` — THE INITIAL DEFAULT STATE — SO THE WHOLE PROCEDURE CAN BE REPEATED.** A stuck or failed cluster load is escaped by the operator, never by a timer (R-4001). Cancel is therefore not "abandon the request": it aborts the in-flight round on every node (the staged work each step built and never published is freed) and drives the cluster to `ClusterState.Idle`, from which the same load can simply be re-issued — the shape `R-210` already gives every load caller (`ScenarioLoadSequence`: Idle first, then the load). ⛔ A cancel that leaves nodes half-loaded, or that leaves the cluster in a `Loading*` state, is the defect this closes. 📐 Measured `2026-10-15`: the abort seam is COMPLETE and never triggered — `ILoadPartProvider.Abort` ("frees whatever `PrepareAsync` built and never published"), `LoadPhaseChain.Abort` (`:206-215`, aborts every step, cancels the hold) and `IClusterStateHandler.Abort` all exist, `ClusterSlave` never calls `Abort`, and `LoadPhaseChain.CanHandle` does not claim `NodeOpType.AbortTransaction` ⇒ the master's abort fan-out finds no handler and auto-ACKs `Success` while nothing rolls back (`ClusterSlave.cs:473-482`) — the silent no-op `R-133` names. ⭐ `TerrainAssetHandler` already claims `AbortTransaction` and is the pattern to copy, not to re-invent. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "if cluster loading fails, operator cancels, cluster goes to the initial default state when the whole procedure can be repeated."** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-H |
| ⭐⭐ **R-4005** | ✅ **A NODE'S ROLE IS WHAT IT ACTUALLY DOES, NEVER A NETWORK FACT — AND A SINGLE-PROCESS HOST IS AN ORDINARY NODE, NOT AN EXCEPTION.** Roles are declared from the capabilities a host composes; they are not derived from, and do not depend on, whether that host has a network participant. An all-in-one host (the editor) declares the UNION of the roles it plays, and every mechanism keyed on a role must work over the internal bus with no network present. ⛔ `NodeRole.None` means "plays no role", never "is not networked". 📐 Measured `2026-10-15`: the editor already declares `Brain|MuscleGround|Perception|NavigationSolver` for its capability plan and its load chain (`EditorCapabilities.cs:60-62`, per the user's `2026-09-07` ruling quoted there) while telling the roster `NodeRole.None` (`EditorSubsystem.cs:1153`) — two producers for one slot (`R-132`), and it is the roster copy that authority, asset-needs and the step barrier read. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "The concepts must stay unified, no exceptions, and editor is just a special case of shared unified generic rules."** · *"Roles are never network oriented, all needs to work using internal bus events even without any network."* | [`../DESIGN_Node_Roles_And_Policies.md`](../DESIGN_Node_Roles_And_Policies.md) §3.4 |
| ⭐⭐ **R-4006** | ✅ **A STALE NODE IS RECOGNISED BY A CLUSTER SESSION ID, AND THE MASTER TRUSTS ONLY WHAT IT WITNESSED.** The master mints a session GUID at process start and publishes it on the durable `ClusterStateTopic`; a node that sees a different session id, or the master gone, drops to `Idle` — its world ended. The master keeps an `InSync` set (a node enters only by ACKing the commit the cluster is at, leaves on ejection); any roster node not `InSync` — new, restarted, back from an outage, or from a dead cluster — is caught up from a reset to `Idle`. `TransactionEpoch` is written, as telemetry only. A stale node's durable samples are cleared by its reset. 📐 Measured `2026-10-15`: no session or incarnation id exists anywhere; node ids are configured, so a restarted node returns under the same id; `TransactionEpoch` (mgmt-1 §4.2) is declared and never written; an ejected node that heartbeats again is silently re-admitted at face value (`ClusterMaster.cs:553,690-700`). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "I-x accepted."** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-I (I1, I2, I4, I5, I6) |
| ⭐⭐ **R-4007** | ✅ **A NODE TAKES IN WORLD STATE ONLY WHEN IT CAN BUILD IT — THROUGH THE EXISTING FREEZE GATE, AND WORLD EVENTS ARE DRAINED.** Every node wires `CycloneNetworkIngressSystem.IsWorldStateFrozen` (today only CGF's debugger halt does) to one node-level predicate: frozen until the node commits a load, frozen again at a reset to `Idle`, frozen while the debugger halts it, frozen while it replays locally. While frozen for a join, world-STATE translators are not polled (DDS keeps the latest sample per entity) and world-EVENT translators (`Volatile + KeepAll`: shots, detonations, hits, requests) are drained and discarded, so no stale burst follows the opening. Control-plane ingress never freezes. ⛔ Not dynamically installed translators, not a global variable. 📐 Measured `2026-10-15`: the gate exists and reaches both ingress paths (`CycloneNetworkIngressSystem.cs:46-66`, `EntityStatesIngressPack.cs:73`); ~20 world-state topics are `Volatile + KeepAll`. ⛔ Prerequisite: `CE-4000`. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "Lean (L2′): accepted. WorldEvents must be drained as you suggest."** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-L L2′ |
| ⭐⭐ **R-4008** | ✅ **AN ENTITY LIVES AS LONG AS ITS PRIMARY OWNER, ON EVERY NODE ALIKE — A PLAYER MAY CREATE ENTITIES LIKE ANY NODE.** No node kind is restricted in what it may create or own; an entity (or a part of it) ends with its owner unless ownership was handed over first (R-177, `DESIGN_Entity_Ownership_Transfer.md`). 📐 Already the measured behaviour: an IG-created tank kept its parts through a SimHost crash (`DESIGN_Ownership_Groups_And_Grants.md` E8), and a creator's crash removes its entities everywhere (live run `2026-10-15`). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "entities (or their portions) die with their owner (creator if ownership not transferred) so it seems nothing actually changes if we allow player node create entities no matter if anything depend on them or not"** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-L L6 |
| ⭐⭐ **R-4009** | ✅ **THE MASTER ASSIGNS NODE IDS UNLESS A NODE FORCES ONE.** Before composing anything that depends on its id, a node process asks (its computer name + process id — ⚠ AMENDED by R-4016, was a node-minted `InstanceId` — forced id or none, preferred id) and waits — its own idle — for the master's grant. Dynamic ids come from their own range and are never reused within a session; a forced id held by a live instance is refused, loudly; the preferred id lets a node keep its id across a new session. On the networkless editor the same request reaches its own in-process master. 📐 Measured `2026-10-15`: the id is baked in at composition (`HrotNodeBuilder.cs:163,232,238-239,251`); two Players launched alike share one id and both obey (`CE-4001`). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "L1 (master-assigned node ids) approved."** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-L L1 |
| ⭐ **R-4010** | ✅ **`CgfRecordReplayController` IS DELETED — DEAD CODE WHOSE OWN ROAD-MAP IS DONE.** Its header calls it a *"Phase 3 skeleton"* that would later *"mirror the EcsRecordReplayController path"*; CGF now builds `EcsRecordReplayController` itself (`CgfApplication.cs:163`) and the skeleton has zero construction sites. ⚠ The `ListenerRecordReplayController` is NOT covered — it is used (IG, ExCon). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "L5 deletion of unused code accepted"** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-L L5 |
| ⭐ **R-4011** | ✅ **ONE RECORD/REPLAY CONTROLLER: `EcsRecordReplayController` ON EVERY ECS HOST, AND NONE ON A HOST WITH NO ECS WORLD (ExCon).** No role gate decides whether an ECS host gets it (today SimHost's bootstrapper builds it only for `Brain|MuscleGround`, `NodeBootstrapper.cs:270-272`, and IG runs a no-op). ExCon records nothing; the replay rounds it does not handle are auto-ACKed (`ClusterSlave.cs:473-482`). ⚠ Before ExCon's `ListenerRecordReplayController` goes, check what on ExCon reads its `IsReplayActive` (the live-from-replay gate in `ReferenceReplayLoadHandler`). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "Ecs as the one controller on every ECS host; no controller on ExCon - approved (just that part)"** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-L L5 |
| ⭐⭐ **R-4012** | ✅ **IN A REPLAY, EACH NODE RESTORES ITS OWN RECORDING FOR THE MOMENTS IT COVERS AND LISTENS OUTSIDE THEM.** Every ECS node records everything (Plan A — the replay design's reasons for rejecting owned-only recording stand: restore is a raw chunk blit and a keyframe clears the world). At each seek and step a node asks only *"is this moment inside my recording's span?"*, answered from its own frame index (`FrameMetadata.WallClockTicks`): inside ⇒ restore locally with world-state ingress frozen (R-4007); outside ⇒ drop what it restored, ingress open, the world from the owners' flood. No role and no orchestration decide it. ⛔ Required change: a seek outside the span must report "not covered" — today it silently restores frame 0 (`PlaybackController.cs:274-275`). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "L3‴ approved"** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-L L3‴ |
| ⭐⭐ **R-4013** | ✅ **A JOINER NEVER GATES THE RUNNING GAME (Q91 J1, J2, J4, J5).** Operator rounds go to the `InSync` set only, never the whole roster; a catch-up is its own per-node saga and never takes the master's single parked slot; the "does not support reliable init" mark is cleared when a node enters `InSync` and when it departs; file transfers may be capped by a token bucket (a setting, off until measured). ⭐ **R-143 EXEMPTION:** the transfer cap paces network I/O on the WALL clock — it decides nothing the simulation computes (the second exemption after R-244). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "J1-J5 accepted. J5 needs wall clock time of course."** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-J |
| ⭐⭐ **R-4014** | ✅ **ONE RULE FOR EVERY GATE, AND NO ENDLESS RETRY (Q91 K6, K7).** An observer never gates anything and a node not in sync never participates — in 2PC rounds, the step barrier and the reliable-init wait-set alike (wait-set = advertises reliable-init ∩ `InSync` ∩ authoritative). A catch-up is tried once per process instance and per cluster commit; a failure is shown on the panel and retried by the operator or by a restart. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "K6: sounds OK" · "K7: OK"** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-K |
| ⭐⭐ **R-4015** | ✅ **A LATE JOINER NEVER READS THE SCENARIO.** A catch-up runs the universal load parts only — knowledge base and terrain (⚠ AMENDED by R-4018: the TKB FILE arrives by the sync every node gets when it joins the cluster, so the catch-up never transfers it; its knowledge-base step only loads that file) — on every node whatever its role, and never creates scenario entities; the world arrives by replication. It matters for a restarted Brain, which would otherwise re-run genesis and duplicate every unit. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "late joiner never reads a scenario as he is late, so he missed the loading" · "K5 approved"** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-K K5′ |
| ⭐⭐ **R-4016** | ✅ **NO RANDOM IDS: A PROCESS IS IDENTIFIED BY ITS COMPUTER NAME + PROCESS ID UNTIL THE MASTER NAMES IT.** The node-id request and grant (R-4009) are matched by facts the process already has — the same ones the DDS binding publishes as `SenderIdentity` — never by an id the node mints. Node ids and the session id come from the master (R-4009, R-4006). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "yes, computer name + process id"** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-L L1 |
| ⭐⭐ **R-4017** | ✅ **THE CATCH-UP IS BUILT FROM PER-NODE OPS ONLY — NEVER BY RE-ISSUING THE OPERATOR'S LOAD.** The master sends the joiner the individual prepare/commit steps addressed to it alone (`FanOutNodeOp`); it never publishes a `TransitionStateIntent` or enters `ProcessTransitionStateIntent` for a catch-up, because that path treats it as a fresh cluster load: it jumps the cluster clock to 0, paused (`GlobalContextProcessManager.cs:44-49`), and resets the network-id authority in a running world (`ClusterMaster.cs:1406-1426`). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "K4 approved"** | [`Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md`](Architect_Question_91_Player_Nodes.md) §4-K K4 |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-4018** | ✅ **ONE TKB PER DEPLOYMENT; A SCENARIO NEVER NAMES IT; IT IS SYNCED FROM ITS MASTER COPY WHEN A NODE JOINS THE CLUSTER AND RE-CHECKED BEFORE EVERY SCENARIO LOAD.** The master copy stays on the NAS where it lives today; its name comes from the orchestrator's configuration. `Header.TkbName` leaves the scenario format — never written, never read; an old file's value is ignored, so a scenario can never be refused over it. When a node joins (first start, restart or late join — one path) the orchestrator copies the TKB into its staging, skipped when the node already holds the same bytes, and the node loads it once and keeps it. The same copy runs before every scenario load as a freshness check: unchanged ⇒ nothing copied or reloaded; changed ⇒ copied and reloaded before the scenario is built. The name travels with the file sync, never with the load command. A late joiner's catch-up transfers no TKB — the join sync already delivered it, and the catch-up's knowledge-base step loads it (amends R-4015). ⛔ SUPERSEDED the same day: *"loaded at process start from an installed copy; the orchestrator stops staging the TKB"* — the user kept the staging. 📐 Measured `2026-10-15`: one scenario file (the `basic-desert` seed) named `HrotDefault` and no TKB archive exists — every node runs on the built-in catalogue (⛔ an earlier "all 10 files" counted build-output copies); the copy is started only by a scenario transition today (`ClusterMaster.cs:1182`, `:1468`). ⭐ The name is a code default, `ClusterConfiguration.TkbName = "HrotDefault"`, working with no configuration file; 🔒 no master copy is legal — nothing is copied and every node keeps its built-in catalogue (user: *"The TKB name in the configuration must default to the single tkb file that we are having now. The configuration should not read any text file. It must work with default settings as is."* · *"Agreed, go ahead with the fallback"*). Build item `CE-4003`. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "If the TKB is mentioned as part of the scenario, I think this is wrong, because keeping multiple TKBs, which are full of technical information, is unmaintainable, so you need to keep just one copy and reuse it everywhere."** · *"We have to keep copying TKB files. It cannot be removed if its master copy is living where it used to live before."* · *"I didn't mean only when the node is late joining, but when the node is loading at all and becoming part of the cluster."* | [`designs/tkb-1/DESIGN.md`](../designs/tkb-1/DESIGN.md) §0 |
| ⭐⭐ **R-4019** | ✅ **ENTERING `Idle` EMPTIES THE WORLD ON EVERY NODE, AND EACH NODE DELETES ON THE WIRE WHAT IT OWNED (`CE-4000` fix F2).** Every route into `Idle` — a normal transition, the operator's cancel (`R-4004`), a node dropping to `Idle` on a new session or a gone master (`R-4006`) — wipes the node's world, and every entity the node owned is disposed on the wire, so a late joiner never receives a dead run. A node ignores a delete whose sender is known and is not the entity's known owner. ⚠ The accepted cost: after Stop the final world cannot be inspected; only replay shows it. ⛔ Rejected F1 (delete at the next load's world boundary): it keeps the dead world alive on the wire through the whole `Idle` period. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "F2 approved."** | [`DESIGN_Cluster_Load_Phase.md`](../DESIGN_Cluster_Load_Phase.md) §8.2 |

The §5 leans are the build basis; a measurement that breaks one stops THAT item (R-106). Instant replays stay out (R-4061). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-21`:** *"lets write a note to backend to implement that"* | [`DESIGN_Film_Director.md`](../DESIGN_Film_Director.md) · [`HANDOFF`](batches/HANDOFF_Coordinator_To_Backend_Film_Director.md) |

| ⭐⭐ **R-4064** | ⛔ **SUPERSEDED FOR STEPPED RUNS by R-3038 (2026-10-21): a film's cuts are scripted in time on a run that repeats.** Kept for a run that cannot repeat (a cluster, live play). ✅ **FILMS ARE PLANNED FOR RUNS THAT DIFFER: a cut fires on an EVENT of the run being shown** (precursors such as a weapon starting to aim give the anticipation; a shot is held until the next event) — never on a time measured on another run; the scout measures how reliably each beat happens; capture retakes a run whose beat is missing; the final films later come from a RECORDING (shoot, scout the recording, edit). Extends backend's R-3034 (every run loads again, never a rewind) and restates the 2026-09-04 canon that scenarios are not deterministic. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-21`:** *"scenarios are not deterministic by design. this might be quite an issue for planning clips"* · *"which might lead to the replay from recording path"* | [`DESIGN_Film_Director.md`](../DESIGN_Film_Director.md) §3d, §3e |

| ⭐ **R-3035** | ✅ **STEPPED MODE IS DETERMINISTIC; LIVE PLAY IS NOT REQUIRED TO BE (CE-3292)**: perception runs in SIM time — never on a paused frame, its clock the sim step (`GlobalTime.FrameNumber`), not the rendered frame. In stepped mode a slow (< 60 Hz) or asynchronous module runs on sim-time slots and its background job is waited for and applied at the next step; a job from before the clock went back (a load, a Stop) is discarded. Live play keeps its frame-counted, CPU-easing cadence. Every run still loads from the file (R-3034). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-21`:** *"perception must run in simtime, for sure"* · *"determinism is only required in stepped mode … the 10Hz and such were meant to be wall clock to ease the CPU"* · *"ok accepted"* | [`DESIGN_Time_Architecture.md`](DESIGN_Time_Architecture.md) §17 |
