| ⭐⭐ **R-3038** | ✅ **A FILM IS SCRIPTED IN TIME ON A STEPPED RUN THAT REPEATS** — a cut, and where the camera looks, happen at the script's time; the scouted run and the filmed run are the same run. ⇒ stepped runs must be deterministic (R-3035), which needs the paused-frame event window (CE-3293) and **ONE STEPPING CADENCE: every run that must repeat — the scout, the live show, the capture — steps EXACTLY ONCE PER RENDERED FRAME**, never zero, never two; real time is kept by holding the frame, not by skipping or doubling steps. Every run still loads from the file (R-3034). Supersedes R-4064's live cues for stepped runs. The film director is built by the **ui lane**. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-21`:** *"it needs to rely on determinism, otherwise we could not specify when what cut should take place and at which time the camera should be at what direction or not."* · *"Yes, write it and write a handoff to the UI line."* | [`DESIGN_Paused_And_Stepped_Execution.md`](DESIGN_Paused_And_Stepped_Execution.md) §10 F1 · [`../DESIGN_Film_Director.md`](../DESIGN_Film_Director.md) §0 |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-3039** | ✅ **A SYSTEM'S WORKING MEMORY IS ENTITIES AND COMPONENTS; A CHECKPOINT IS A PLAIN ECS SNAPSHOT.** A system keeps its memory in components on the entity it is about (a singleton component for world-wide state), every frame — no private copy, no per-system save/load. A checkpoint, a recording and Stop's snapshot copy the world and nothing else; `DataPolicy` is the one knob for what each keeps. A system may hold only DERIVED data (caches, engine objects rebuilt from components); protocol state lives in `Transient` components and is re-derived at a restore. The test per field: empty, with the world restored, does the run continue as if nothing happened? Supersedes CE-3297's "move when touched" and CE-3298's counter lean. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-22`:** *"checkpointing feature solely relying on all non-auto-recoverable state saved in ecs components"* · *"Shoukdnt the syatems use entities ans component as their working memory?"* · *"Yes write the ruling and summarize what needs changing"* | [`DESIGN_Paused_And_Stepped_Execution.md`](DESIGN_Paused_And_Stepped_Execution.md) §7g |
| ⭐⭐ **R-3040** | ✅ **THE DRY RUN AND THE CHECKPOINT SAVE AND RESTORE WITH THE FLIGHT RECORDER — NOT `SyncFrom`.** At Play a keyframe of the live world is kept in RAM, at Stop it is applied (`PlaybackSystem`), then protocol state re-derives; a checkpoint is a keyframe written to a file; a restore is the replay load + go live. One mechanism for dry run, checkpoint and replay; `SyncFrom` stays a background-solver tool. Restore time at the end of a dry run is accepted. Supersedes mgmt-1 §9.3's dry-run mechanics. ⭐ **The split is by frequency:** background-module snapshots run every frame and stay on `SyncFrom`, minimal and maximally fast (never widened to copy every singleton); the rare operations — dry run enter/exit, checkpoint save/restore — use the recorder, complete over fast. 🔒 *"This is not something what is happening every frame, while the background snapshots, they are happening very often. They must be optimized."* 🔒 **User, `2026-10-22`:** *"The restoration time at the end of editor's dry run is not a big issue. For sure, reuse the flight recorder mechanics for dry run save and dry run restore."* | [`DESIGN_Paused_And_Stepped_Execution.md`](DESIGN_Paused_And_Stepped_Execution.md) §7g.0 R0″ · [`../designs/mgmt-1/DESIGN.md`](../designs/mgmt-1/DESIGN.md) §9.3 |
| ⭐ **R-3041** | ✅ **NO NETWORK BUFFERS ARE CAPTURED BY A CHECKPOINT OR A DRY RUN; WHAT WAS IN FLIGHT IS LOST, ACCEPTED.** After a restore, state resyncs because every owner republishes (egress "already sent" cleared); a one-shot message in flight at the save never arrives. Withdraws mgmt-1 §9.1's `.dds` supplement (never built). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-22`:** *"No capturing of network buffers. accepting the losses."* | [`DESIGN_Paused_And_Stepped_Execution.md`](DESIGN_Paused_And_Stepped_Execution.md) §7g.0b · [`../designs/mgmt-1/DESIGN.md`](../designs/mgmt-1/DESIGN.md) §9.1 |
| ⭐⭐ **R-3042** | ✅ **A CHECKPOINT SAVE NEVER STOPS OR PAUSES THE CLUSTER AND NEVER INTERFERES WITH THE RUNNING SIMULATION; CHECKPOINTS MAY BE TAKEN PERIODICALLY.** Like the flight recorder, which records every frame without stopping anything. ⇒ a checkpoint is a TIME (a mark) in the running recording; the state at that time is already recorded. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-22`:** *"Saving a checkpoint cannot stop a cluster. Checkpoint is something that happens while everything else is running. The system can be creating checkpoints on the fly at regular intervals. and it should not interfere with the running simulation. The similar is like flight recorder also doesn't stop the cluster just to record state every frame."* | [`../DESIGN_Checkpoint_And_Dry_Run_Restore.md`](../DESIGN_Checkpoint_And_Dry_Run_Restore.md) §0, D3 |
| ⭐ **R-3043** | ✅ **RESTORING A CHECKPOINT KEEPS WHAT IS ALREADY LOADED** — it does not reload from scratch. ⇒ a restore is a seek of every node's own recording (singletons — terrain, TKB, navmesh — stay); only a fresh process loads, and the load skips what is resident. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-22`:** *"Restoring a checkpoint is allowed, but it shouldn't reload everything from scratch. What is already loaded should stay loaded."* | [`../DESIGN_Checkpoint_And_Dry_Run_Restore.md`](../DESIGN_Checkpoint_And_Dry_Run_Restore.md) §0, D10 |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-3044** | ✅ **A CHECKPOINT IS A NAMED KEYFRAME THE FLIGHT RECORDER WRITES ON REQUEST; A RESTORE SEEKS EVERY NODE STRAIGHT TO IT.** No separate checkpoint stream, writer or format: the master asks every node for *keyframe X at wall time T*; each recorder writes it at its first frame at or after T, labelled; a restore applies keyframe X alone, then the after-restore step. Basis: the recorder records the FULL ECS state (replay-and-modules §2.3, "Plan A — Record Everything"); the only exclusion is a `NoReplay`/`Transient` marking (restore design D1). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-22`:** *"So whenever we need to save a checkpoint, we could basically just force the flight recorder to write a keyframe."* · *"I think to design doesn't prevent saving everything, and it is what is already done, so I think that holds. So I think yes, please write this as a ruling."* | [`../DESIGN_Checkpoint_And_Dry_Run_Restore.md`](../DESIGN_Checkpoint_And_Dry_Run_Restore.md) §0 |
| ⭐⭐ **R-3045** | ✅ **THE FLIGHT RECORDER HAS A CHECKPOINTING MODE — KEYFRAMES ONLY, EVERY N SECONDS OF SIMULATION TIME, NO PER-FRAME DELTAS; THERE IS NO CHECKPOINT MECHANISM OUTSIDE THE FLIGHT RECORDER.** Full recording stays the default; the checkpointing mode is chosen per session where restore points are enough (no smooth replay). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-22`:** *"I was also thinking about some optional lightweight flight recording mode, which is saving periodically just keyframes …"* · *"It's basically a checkpointing mode - better name that might fit better the situation. Yes, recorded as D17."* | [`../DESIGN_Checkpoint_And_Dry_Run_Restore.md`](../DESIGN_Checkpoint_And_Dry_Run_Restore.md) §0, D17 |
| ⭐ **R-3046** | ✅ **THE RESTORE DESIGN'S LEANS D1–D4, D6–D12, D14–D16 ARE APPROVED** (D5 corrected — both the dry-run Stop and a checkpoint restore move the clock with `SnapTo`, never from the keyframe; D13 withdrawn — no in-recording trim exists). 🔒 **User, `2026-10-22`:** *"The other leans accepted."* | [`../DESIGN_Checkpoint_And_Dry_Run_Restore.md`](../DESIGN_Checkpoint_And_Dry_Run_Restore.md) §5 |
| ⭐⭐ **R-3047** | ✅ **GOING LIVE FROM A REPLAY (AND SO A CHECKPOINT RESTORE) NEVER OVERWRITES HISTORY: THE MASTER MINTS A NEW EXERCISE ID, EACH NODE COPIES ITS RECORDING UP TO THE BRANCH POINT INTO IT AND CONTINUES LIVE THERE.** The copy is a byte copy of the frames up to the branch time; the new recording names its parent; the recorder never overwrites an existing file (`CreateNew`). Every exercise is self-contained. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-22`:** *"Wait, so if we are going to live from a replay, does it mean that we copy the history up to the point where we are switching to live and continuing with the live in this new copy of the recording under new exercise ID? That would be okay."* · *"Yes"* (to recording D18 that way) | [`../DESIGN_Checkpoint_And_Dry_Run_Restore.md`](../DESIGN_Checkpoint_And_Dry_Run_Restore.md) D18 · [`Blueprint_Issues_Tracker.md`](Blueprint_Issues_Tracker.md) CE-3300 |
| ⭐ **R-3048** | ✅ **R-143 EXEMPTION (the third, after R-244 and R-4013): A TRAFFIC SOURCE'S SILENCE IS TIMED IN WALL SECONDS THAT RUN ONLY WHILE CLUSTER TIME ADVANCES.** `TrafficModules.SilenceClock` (CE-4021) is a network liveness timeout: it decides when a dead traffic node's replicas are removed, never how a car moves. Sim time alone failed (IG's slave clock corrects in jumps); wall time alone would drop every car during a pause. ⛔ Not a licence for wall-clock simulation timing. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-22`:** *"you lean about R-143 accepted."* | [`Architect_Question_93_Sumo_Traffic_Node.md`](Architect_Question_93_Sumo_Traffic_Node.md) §12.5 |
| ⭐ **R-4003** | ✅ **IG RECORDS LIKE EVERY ECS HOST — UNIFICATION OVER OPTIMISATION.** "IG carries no ECS frame data, so it must not record" is a PRE-UNIFICATION premise and is SUPERSEDED: IG can own its own entities, so it runs the same recording (`EcsRecordReplayController`) and holds the same archive handler as every ECS host. If recording is ever switched off on a node, that is by ROLE or an optimisation setting (R-4000), never by a missing handler. R-140 (IG owns no SCENARIO-persistable entity) is untouched. 🔒 **User, `2026-10-15`: "IG can create its own entities if it wants to. So it might need to run its own recording. I prefer the unification over optimization at this stage, still the disabling would be controlled by the role or by some optimization settings later."** | [`../DESIGN_Node_Roles_And_Policies.md`](../DESIGN_Node_Roles_And_Policies.md) §7.1 |
| ⭐⭐⭐ **R-139** | ⛔⛔⛔ **NO LEAN WITHOUT A CLAIM TABLE — and STAYING QUIET LONGER IS THE POINT.** 🔒 **User, `2026-09-01`:** *"we are constatly hitting the point from the beginning - your understanding is shallw before giving lean. hiw to improve?"* ⇒ 🔒 *"staying quiet longer to give much better answer is exactly what i expect."* ⭐⭐ **The full rule lives in `.claude/CLAUDE.md`** *("NO LEAN WITHOUT A CLAIM TABLE")* — the only reliable auto-loaded channel; ⭐ **indexed here so a ledger-first reader still finds it.** ⛔⛔ **The diagnosis is NOT "didn't read enough"** — 📐 plenty was read. ⭐⭐⭐ **The mechanism: the RECOMMENDATION WAS FORMED FIRST and the reading went looking for support**, so every lean rested on one verified property plus several ASSUMED ones — **and the assumed ones decided the question.** 📌 Measured on `CE-147` *(where the `NetworkTransform` position-shadow attach belongs)*: **three leans, three corrections, each broken by a file nameable in advance as load-bearing and never opened** *(the component's own header · `NetworkSpawningSystem.cs:134`'s translator loop sitting ABOVE the authority branch · `EntityQuery.cs:133` being an INDEX walk, which falsified a hazard I had INVENTED)*. ⭐⭐⭐ **The tell: a PRINCIPLE where a `file:line` should be** — *"the consumer should own its cache"* feels strong and is unfalsifiable until the file is opened. ⭐⭐ **The cost asymmetry:** measuring is 2–3 tool calls (~30 s); a wrong lean is a user round-trip plus the risk they ACCEPT it ⇒ **the threshold for measuring is near zero.** ⇒ ⭐⭐⭐ **the artefact: a claim table in the reply, each row ✅`file:line` or ⛔ assumed — and NO ⛔ ROW MAY BE LOAD-BEARING.** ⚠ **Why a table and not "be careful": the user is on mobile and cannot check the reasoning — but they CAN check four-measured vs one.** ⛔ **The pattern being ended is *first lean wrong, third lean right*, i.e. the USER'S CORRECTIONS DOING THE MEASUREMENT** — ⚠ the user acting as the test suite. | `.claude/CLAUDE.md` |


## ⛔⛔⛔ §M — MEASURE, DON'T MEMORISE *(added `2026-08-18`, user ruling)*

> ⭐⭐⭐ **NOTHING IN THIS FILE MAY ASSERT WHAT THE CODE CURRENTLY IS.**
>
> 📌 **Why this section exists.** `rulings-check.py` verifies that **a quote still exists in a
> document**. ⛔⛔ **It CANNOT detect that a claim about CODE became false — because the document did
> not change, the CODE did.** ⚠⚠ **Twice on `2026-08-18` a row was GREEN AND FALSE:** `R-04`
> *("the tagged type is the VIEW")* and `R-25` *("`B′` is blocked")*. ⭐ **Both sent me to build things
> that already existed.**
>
> ⇒ ⭐⭐ **A perishable claim is not canon. It is a QUESTION plus the command that answers it.**
> ⛔ **Never quote an answer from here. Run the command.**

| # | the measurement | ⭐ run this · answer | last |

A probe **clicked a button and typed `42` into an `InputText`** under Xvfb: `clicks=1 typed='42'`. ⭐⭐ **Two mechanics matter:** ① **inject AFTER `rlImGui.Begin()`**, i.e. after the backend has pushed real input, or it is overwritten; ② ⭐⭐⭐ **never guess coordinates — the APP publishes them**: `GetItemRectMin/Max` right after drawing a widget gives its rect in the same space the injection uses ⇒ **coordinate-free and DPI-proof by construction**, ⛔ not "DPI-aware maths". ⚠⚠ **What it CANNOT catch:** injection enters at the ImGui layer, so it **bypasses the OS/polling input path** — 📌 exactly where the user's own dropped-click bug lived. ⚠ And `ManagedWindow.Render` still dies under a real GL context on a zero-handle icon atlas *(Batch 100)*, so **full-window** driving needs that first | `2026-08-21` |

| ⛔⛔⛔ **M-41** | ⭐⭐⭐ **"TWO notions" was wrong and so was "five" — how many are there, and WHO DRAINS a staged write?** *(the user's own measurement, `2026-08-21`: "livewrite unavailable, simup true, frozen true => paused" — ⭐ **the run state was CORRECT and the edit was still refused**)* | `search_graph(name_pattern=".*(IsPaused\|IsFrozen\|IsStopped\|PausedBy).*")` — ⛔ **not grep, `R-74`**; then `trace_path("DataBreakpointManager.DrainPendingMutations", direction=inbound)`; then corroborate with `grep -rn "RequestContinue\|RequestStep" --include=*.cs Hrot/ FDP/ \| grep -v Tests` | ⛔⛔ **`2026-08-21`: TWELVE production notions, not two and not five** *(total 91 declarations)* — enumerated in **`Q48` §0**. ⚠⚠ **And the far bigger finding: `DrainPendingMutations` has exactly THREE production callers — `RequestStep`, `RequestContinue`, `OnHotReloadBegin`, ALL on `DataBreakpointManager` itself**, and **no production code outside that class calls either request** *(graph AND grep agree)*. ⇒ ⭐⭐⭐ **`BlueprintDebugSession.Continue()` resumes via `_timeController.RequestResume()` and never tells the queue** ⇒ ⛔⛔ **a staged live write appears to land only when a HOT RELOAD happens to occur.** ⇒ ⚠ **widening the `_isPaused` gate ALONE would turn "refused with a wrong reason" into "accepted and silently discarded"** — 📌 `Q48-C` is therefore the BLOCKING sub-question, not `Q48-A`. ⛔ **Reading only — no rail has run this end to end; `Q48-E` is what would** | `2026-08-21` |

The only real reason might be threading issues")* | read `SubsystemOrchestrator:105-114`; then `ExecutionPolicy.Validate:148-157` and `ModuleHostKernel:246`; then `ModuleHostKernel.ShouldRunThisFrame` — ⚠ **does it consult `deltaTime`?** | ⭐⭐ **`2026-08-21`: THREADING IS NOT THE REASON.** 📐 The runner is ONE loop *(`Update(dt); DrawWorldAll(); DrawUIAll();`)*, `DataStrategy.Direct` is `Synchronous`-only and **enforced** by `ExecutionPolicy.Validate`, and async modules run on **leased views** ⇒ ⭐ **the UI writes between frames with nothing else touching the live repo.** ⛔⛔ **The real mechanism: `ShouldRunThisFrame` NEVER CONSULTS `deltaTime`** ⇒ a module at ≥60 Hz **ticks every frame even while paused**, with `moduleDelta == 0` ⇒ ⭐⭐⭐ **a direct write to a RECOMPUTED value is overwritten on the next frame, with time still stopped.** ⇒ ⭐ **STAGE and drain at the top of an ADVANCING tick** — ⚠ **and accept that a recomputed variable cannot SHOW its new value while paused**; the surface must say *queued*, not pretend. 📌 The old refusal sentence *"the edit would be overwritten by the next tick"* described a REAL mechanism — ⛔ refusing instead of staging was the error | `2026-08-21` |

## 4. ⭐⭐ WHERE TO LOOK when there is no row

> ⭐⭐⭐ **USER, `2026-08-17`:** *"most designs are in the **docs** folder. in the `.dev` those named like
> 'design' describe **what was implemented**."* ⭐⭐⭐ **REFINED `2026-08-21`, verbatim:** *"the .dev
> contains implemented intents, but they are still intents telling often more than the code."*
> ⇒ ⛔⛔ **CORRECTION of my earlier over-reading:** I had written *".dev is as-built, citing it proves
> nothing." That was WRONG.* ⭐⭐ **A `.dev` design is an IMPLEMENTED intent — it explains the WHY and
> the MEANT-TO-BE, often more than the code does.** ⇒ **it IS a valid intent source.** ⚠ The only
> caveat: it describes work already built, so **check whether a NEWER `docs/` design supersedes it**
> *(STATUS block / recency)* — ⛔ not *"discard it because it is old."*

| # | look | it tells you |
|---|---|---|
| ① | ⭐⭐⭐ **`docs/**` — `Architect_Question_*_ANSWERS.md`** | ⭐ **THE RULINGS.** ⛔ the non-`ANSWERS` files carry only options |
| ② | ⭐⭐ **their §"Sequencing" tables** | ⛔ **a finding with a planned batch is NOT a new finding** |
| ③ | ⭐⭐ **`docs/` — `DESIGN_*.md`, `*_Unification.md`, `BOOTSTRAP_*.md`, `PLAN_*.md`, `docs/UX/`, `docs/projects/`** | ⭐ **THE CURRENT INTENT — the model as it is MEANT to be now** |
| ④ | ⭐ **`.dev/<programme>/*-DESIGN.md`, `*_Detailed_Design.md`** | ⭐⭐ **IMPLEMENTED INTENT — the WHY behind what was built, often more than the code.** ⚠ check for a newer `docs/` supersession before treating it as current |
| ⑤ | `.dev/**/reports/*-REPORT.md` tails · `TASK-DETAIL.md` | **the DEBT** *(`DEBT-*` ids are filed here and nowhere else)* · the authorising user decision |
| ⛔ | `*-INSTRUCTIONS.md`, `reviews/*`, `batches/` | ⛔ **ephemera — they restate the design; not an intent source** |

⭐⭐ **The distinction that stays:** ⛔ **CODE** tells you *how it IS* and can never tell you *how it was
MEANT to be*; ⭐ **a `.dev` design doc, even an old one, DOES carry intent the code cannot.** ⚠ **What a
`.dev` doc cannot promise is CURRENCY** — a later `docs/` design may have moved on. ⇒ read it for intent,
check its STATUS for supersession.
